opts)\n\n\tw, _ := doRequest(mw, tok)\n\tif w.Code != http.StatusUnauthorized {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"status = %d, want 401 for a mismatched issuer\", w.Code)\n\t}\n}\n\n// TestJWTMiddleware_AlgNone_401 is RFC 8725 §3.1's classic attack: a\n// token whose header claims alg:none, carrying no signature at all. The\n// algorithm-allowlist passed to jwt.ParseWithClaims must reject this\n// before ever reaching the keyfunc/signature-verification step.\nfunc TestJWTMiddleware_AlgNone_401(t *testing.T) {\n\tmw := newTestMiddleware(map[string]*rsa.PublicKey{})\n\n\tunsignedClaims := claims{\n\t\tScope: string(ScopeSearch),\n\t\tRegisteredClaims: jwt.RegisteredClaims{\n\t\t\tIssuer:    testIssuer,\n\t\t\tSubject:   \"attacker\",\n\t\t\tAudience:  jwt.ClaimStrings{testAudience},\n\t\t\tExpiresAt: jwt.NewNumericDate(time.Now().Add(10 * time.Minute)),\n\t\t},\n\t}\n\ttok := jwt.NewWithClaims(jwt.SigningMethodNone, unsignedClaims)\n\tsigned, err := tok.SignedString(jwt.UnsafeAllowNoneSignatureType)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"constructing alg:none token: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tw, _ := doRequest(mw, signed)\n\tif w.Code != http.StatusUnauthorized {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"status = %d, want 401 for an alg:none token\", w.Code)\n\t}\n}\n\n// TestJWTMiddleware_HMACConfusionWithPublicKeyAsSecret_401 is RFC 8725\n// §3.1's other classic attack: an attacker who knows the RSA public key\n// (public by definition — it's published as JWKS) signs a token with\n// HS256 using the PEM-encoded public key bytes as the HMAC secret. A\n// verifier that blindly uses whatever alg the token header claims, and\n// hands the \"key\" returned by its keyfunc to that algorithm's verifier,\n// gets fooled: HS256 with the public key as the secret \"verifies\"\n// successfully against a keyfunc that returns the RSA public key\n// unconditionally. The algorithm allowlist must reject this the same way\n// it rejects alg:none.\nfunc TestJWTMiddleware_HMACConfusionWithPublicKeyAsSecret_401(t *testing.T) {\n\tkey := generateRSAKeyPair(t)\n\tmw := newTestMiddleware(map[string]*rsa.PublicKey{\"key-1\": &key.PublicKey})\n\n\tpubDER, err := x509MarshalPKCS1PublicKey(&key.PublicKey)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"marshaling public key: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tforged := claims{\n\t\tScope: string(ScopeSearch),\n\t\tRegisteredClaims: jwt.RegisteredClaims{\n\t\t\tIssuer:    testIssuer,\n\t\t\tSubject:   \"attacker\",\n\t\t\tAudience:  jwt.ClaimStrings{testAudience},\n\t\t\tExpiresAt: jwt.NewNumericDate(time.Now().Add(10 * time.Minute)),\n\t\t},\n\t}\n\ttok := jwt.NewWithClaims(jwt.SigningMethodHS256, forged)\n\ttok.Header[\"kid\"] = \"key-1\"\n\tsigned, err := tok.SignedString(pubDER)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"signing HMAC-confusion token: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tw, _ := doRequest(mw, signed)\n\tif w.Code != http.StatusUnauthorized {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"status = %d, want 401 for an HS256-with-public-key-as-secret forged token\", w.Code)\n\t}\n}\n\n// TestJWTMiddleware_KidRotation_BothKeysVerify is the acceptance\n// criterion for key rotation: two tokens signed under two different kids\n// both verify successfully when both keys are configured, supporting a\n// rotation overlap window.\nfunc TestJWTMiddleware_KidRotation_BothKeysVerify(t *testing.T) {\n\toldKey := generateRSAKeyPair(t)\n\tnewKey := generateRSAKeyPair(t)\n\tmw := newTestMiddleware(map[string]*rsa.PublicKey{\n\t\t\"key-old\": &oldKey.PublicKey,\n\t\t\"key-new\": &newKey.PublicKey,\n\t})\n\n\toldTok := signToken(t, oldKey, defaultOpts(\"key-old\"))\n\tnewTok := signToken(t, newKey, defaultOpts(\"key-new\"))\n\n\tif w, _ := doRequest(mw, oldTok); w.Code != http.StatusOK {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"old-kid token status = %d, want 200 during rotation overlap\", w.Code)\n\t}\n\tif w, _ := doRequest(mw, newTok); w.Code != http.StatusOK {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"new-kid token status = %d, want 200 during rotation overlap\", w.Code)\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc TestJWTMiddleware_UnknownKid_401(t *testing.T) {\n\tkey := generateRSAKeyPair(t)\n\tmw := newTestMiddleware(map[string]*rsa.PublicKey{\"key-1\": &key.PublicKey})\n\ttok := signToken(t, key, defaultOpts(\"key-does-not-exist\"))\n\n\tw, _ := doRequest(mw, tok)\n\tif w.Code != http.StatusUnauthorized {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"status = %d, want 401 for an unrecognized kid\", w.Code)\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc TestJWTMiddleware_WrongKeyForKid_401(t *testing.T) {\n\tsigningKey := generateRSAKeyPair(t)\n\totherKey := generateRSAKeyPair(t)\n\t// The keyfunc returns otherKey's public key for \"key-1\" — simulating\n\t// a mismatched/corrupted JWKS entry. Signature verification must fail.\n\tmw := newTestMiddleware(map[string]*rsa.PublicKey{\"key-1\": &otherKey.PublicKey})\n\ttok := signToken(t, signingKey, defaultOpts(\"key-1\"))\n\n\tw, _ := doRequest(mw, tok)\n\tif w.Code != http.StatusUnauthorized {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"status = %d, want 401 when the signature doesn't verify against the returned key\", w.Code)\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc TestJWTMiddleware_NoScopeClaim_401(t *testing.T) {\n\tkey := generateRSAKeyPair(t)\n\tmw := newTestMiddleware(map[string]*rsa.PublicKey{\"key-1\": &key.PublicKey})\n\topts := defaultOpts(\"key-1\")\n\topts.scope = \"\"\n\ttok := signToken(t, key, opts)\n\n\tw, _ := doRequest(mw, tok)\n\tif w.Code != http.StatusUnauthorized {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"status = %d, want 401 for a token with no recognized scope\", w.Code)\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc TestJWTMiddleware_MultipleScopes_401(t *testing.T) {\n\tkey := generateRSAKeyPair(t)\n\tmw := newTestMiddleware(map[string]*rsa.PublicKey{\"key-1\": &key.PublicKey})\n\topts := defaultOpts(\"key-1\")\n\topts.scope = string(ScopeSearch) + \" \" + string(ScopeReadOwn)\n\ttok := signToken(t, key, httptest.NewRecorder()\n\th.ServeHTTP(w, req)\n\tif !sawDeadline {\n\t\tt.Error(\"request context should carry a deadline after NewDeadlineMiddleware\")\n\t}\n}\n"

1	// Package app is the shared bootstrap both cmd/api-service and
2	// cmd/idp-connector wrap thinly, per decisions/go-layout-debate.md's ruling:
3	// route registration and config wiring live once here, not duplicated per
4	// binary. It carries no behavior beyond wiring — service-specific handlers
5	// belong in internal/api and internal/connector as those stories land.
6	package app
7	
8	import (
9		"encoding/json"
10		"log"
11		"net/http"
12	)
13	
14	// healthResponse is the JSON body every health probe returns. Field set is
15	// deliberately minimal per 02's ruling (api-auth-design.md) on this
16	// unauthenticated endpoint: an up/down status, which service answered, and
17	// an opaque build identifier (commit SHA) — never a version string, build
18	// timestamp, connection detail, or anything else. Do not add fields here
19	// without checking that ruling first; see refinement/LT-34.md.
20	type healthResponse struct {
21		Status  string `json:"status"`
22		Service string `json:"service"`
23		Build   string `json:"build"`
24	}
25	
26	// Mode is api-service's run mode (LT-32). A defined type rather than a
27	// bare string so NewRouter's switch below can have a real default case —
28	// config.Load already validates APP_MODE to exactly "verifier"/"issuer"
29	// before it reaches here, but NewRouter must not depend on that: the whole
30	// point of this story is that a misconfiguration is loud, not silently
31	// downgraded, and a router that quietly falls back to verifier behavior on
32	// any unrecognized value (a case-mismatch, a stray space) is the same
33	// failure class one layer up (Oren Castellan, PR #18 review).
34	type Mode string
35	
36	// ModeVerifier and ModeIssuer are api-service's two run modes, selected by
37	// config.Config.AppMode and passed to NewRouter by cmd/api-service/main.go.
38	// idp-connector has no issuer/verifier split — its main.go passes
39	// ModeVerifier, the only mode that adds no route beyond /healthz, since
40	// that value is also this package's harmless default.
41	const (
42		ModeVerifier Mode = "verifier"
43		ModeIssuer   Mode = "issuer"
44	)
45	
46	// NewRouter builds the http.Handler shared by both binaries' main.go.
47	// service identifies which binary is answering ("api-service" or
48	// "idp-connector") in the health response, per LT-34's acceptance
49	// criteria. mode is api-service's APP_MODE (LT-32) — the router itself is
50	// this story's startup-time branch point named in refinement/LT-32.md's
51	// acceptance criteria ("internal/app branches on it at startup"): in
52	// ModeIssuer, a placeholder route is registered at the path reserved for
53	// token issuance so the issuer and verifier Deployments are demonstrably
54	// different routers, not just different env vars read by code that
55	// otherwise behaves identically. The actual issuance handler is S7/LT-40's
56	// scope, not this story's (refinement/LT-32.md's non-goals) — this only
57	// proves the wiring exists and is reachable on the Deployment that is
58	// supposed to have it, and absent on the one that isn't.
59	//
60	// An unrecognized mode logs loudly and falls back to the verifier route
61	// set — the safer of the two failure directions (an issuer Deployment
62	// stuck without its route is externally visible as 404s on /auth/token,
63	// while a verifier Deployment that accidentally grew the issuer route
64	// would be the actual security regression this story exists to prevent)
65	// — rather than panicking a running server over a value config.Load
66	// should already have rejected before this ever runs.
67	func NewRouter(service string, mode Mode) http.Handler {
68		mux := http.NewServeMux()
69		mux.HandleFunc("/healthz", healthHandler(service))
70		switch mode {
71		case ModeIssuer:
72			mux.HandleFunc("/auth/token", issuerPlaceholderHandler)
73		case ModeVerifier:
74			// no additional routes
75		default:
76			log.Printf("app: NewRouter called with unrecognized mode %q — serving verifier routes only", mode)
77		}
78		return mux
79	}
80	
81	// issuerPlaceholderHandler exists only to make the issuer/verifier router
82	// split observable and testable before S7/LT-40 implements real token
83	// issuance. It deliberately does no cryptography and holds no reference to
84	// config.Config.JWTSigningKeyPath — that wiring belongs to whichever S7
85	// handler replaces this one.
86	func issuerPlaceholderHandler(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
87		http.Error(w, "token issuance not yet implemented — reserved for LT-40", http.StatusNotImplemented)
88	}
89	
90	func healthHandler(service string) http.HandlerFunc {
91		return func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
92			w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
93			w.WriteHeader(http.StatusOK)
94			_ = json.NewEncoder(w).Encode(healthResponse{
95				Status:  "ok",
96				Service: service,
97				Build:   Commit,
98			})
99		}
100	}

1	// Package config loads the env-var configuration surface published in root
2	// PLANNING.md's Service boundaries section. Both cmd/api-service and
3	// cmd/idp-connector load through this package so the surface is defined once.
4	package config
5	
6	import (
7		"fmt"
8		"os"
9		"strings"
10	)
11	
12	// Config holds every environment variable named in PLANNING.md's Service
13	// boundaries section. Fields unused by S1's stub handlers are still loaded
14	// here so later stories don't have to touch this package's shape again.
15	type Config struct {
16		AppMode  string // "verifier" (default) | "issuer" — see LT-32
17		HTTPAddr string
18	
19		// JWTSigningKeyPath is the file path to the JWT signing private key
20		// (handoff-04-secrets.md row #2 — "runtime-injected as a file, never
21		// an env var"). Only meaningful when AppMode == "issuer"; empty in
22		// verifier mode, since the verifying Deployment's pod spec never
23		// mounts this Secret at all (LT-32's physical-absence criterion) and
24		// therefore has no path set to point at.
25		JWTSigningKeyPath string
26	
27		DBDriver string // postgres | cockroachdb | sqlite
28		DBDSN    string // resolved value: DB_DSN_FILE content takes precedence over DB_DSN
29	
30		AuthJWTIssuer   string
31		AuthJWTAudience string
32	
33		ConnectorJWTAudience  string
34		ConnectorClientID     string
35		ConnectorClientSecret string
36	
37		IDPABCBaseURL      string
38		IDPABCClientID     string
39		IDPABCClientSecret string
40	}
41	
42	// Load reads the process environment into a Config. DB_DSN_FILE, when set,
43	// is read and its trimmed content used as DBDSN in preference to DB_DSN —
44	// DB_DSN is a transitional fallback, not a permanent equal option, per
45	// handoff-04-secrets.md's S7 revision.
46	//
47	// APP_MODE is validated, not merely defaulted: an unrecognized value is a
48	// startup error rather than a silent fall-through to "verifier", because a
49	// typo'd issuer Deployment silently running as a verifier (or vice versa)
50	// is exactly the class of misconfiguration LT-32 exists to make loud
51	// instead of invisible.
52	//
53	// In issuer mode, JWT_SIGNING_KEY_FILE must be set and the file it names
54	// must actually be readable, checked here at startup rather than deferred
55	// to whatever first tries to use it. This is LT-32's defense-in-depth
56	// criterion: the verifying Deployment's pod spec never mounts this Secret
57	// at all (enforced on 04's side, by physical absence, not by this check),
58	// so if APP_MODE were ever misconfigured to "issuer" on that Deployment,
59	// this still fails startup rather than serving traffic in a half-issuer
60	// state with no actual path to the private key.
61	func Load() (Config, error) {
62		dsn, err := resolveDSN()
63		if err != nil {
64			return Config{}, err
65		}
66	
67		appMode := os.Getenv("APP_MODE")
68		if appMode == "" {
69			appMode = "verifier"
70		}
71		if appMode != "verifier" && appMode != "issuer" {
72			return Config{}, fmt.Errorf("config: APP_MODE %q is not one of \"verifier\", \"issuer\"", appMode)
73		}
74	
75		signingKeyPath, err := resolveSigningKeyPath(appMode)
76		if err != nil {
77			return Config{}, err
78		}
79	
80		return Config{
81			AppMode:           appMode,
82			JWTSigningKeyPath: signingKeyPath,
83			HTTPAddr:          os.Getenv("HTTP_ADDR"),
84	
85			DBDriver: os.Getenv("DB_DRIVER"),
86			DBDSN:    dsn,
87	
88			AuthJWTIssuer:   os.Getenv("AUTH_JWT_ISSUER"),
89			AuthJWTAudience: os.Getenv("AUTH_JWT_AUDIENCE"),
90	
91			ConnectorJWTAudience:  os.Getenv("CONNECTOR_JWT_AUDIENCE"),
92			ConnectorClientID:     os.Getenv("CONNECTOR_CLIENT_ID"),
93			ConnectorClientSecret: os.Getenv("CONNECTOR_CLIENT_SECRET"),
94	
95			IDPABCBaseURL:      os.Getenv("IDP_ABC_BASE_URL"),
96			IDPABCClientID:     os.Getenv("IDP_ABC_CLIENT_ID"),
97			IDPABCClientSecret: os.Getenv("IDP_ABC_CLIENT_SECRET"),
98		}, nil
99	}
100	
101	// resolveSigningKeyPath enforces LT-32's issuer-mode requirement: in
102	// "issuer" mode, JWT_SIGNING_KEY_FILE must name a regular file this
103	// process can actually read at least one byte of, checked now rather than
104	// left for whatever code first tries to sign a token — a startup failure
105	// here is the only thing that distinguishes "issuer mode with no key"
106	// from "issuer mode with a key", and the whole point of the
107	// physical-absence criterion is that those two must never be confused for
108	// one another. In "verifier" mode the variable is not read at all: the
109	// verifying binary has no legitimate use for a signing-key path, set or
110	// not.
111	//
112	// This checks openable-and-non-empty at startup, not "will still be a
113	// valid key when S7 reads it" — a Secret rotated to empty or a volume
114	// re-provisioned later isn't something a one-time startup check can see
115	// (Nolan Reyes, PR #18 review). That's S7's problem to solve when it
116	// exists, not this story's: LT-32 only owns making a *missing* key loud
117	// at startup, not monitoring a *present* key's ongoing validity.
118	func resolveSigningKeyPath(appMode string) (string, error) {
119		if appMode != "issuer" {
120			return "", nil
121		}
122		path := os.Getenv("JWT_SIGNING_KEY_FILE")
123		if path == "" {
124			return "", fmt.Errorf("config: APP_MODE=issuer requires JWT_SIGNING_KEY_FILE to be set")
125		}
126		fi, err := os.Stat(path)
127		if err != nil {
128			return "", fmt.Errorf("config: APP_MODE=issuer set but JWT_SIGNING_KEY_FILE %q is not accessible: %w", path, err)
129		}
130		// os.Open succeeds on a directory as readily as on a regular file —
131		// no error, nothing distinguishing the two. JWT_SIGNING_KEY_FILE
132		// pointed at a mount directory instead of the file inside it (an easy
133		// Kubernetes subPath-vs-mount-root mistake, and exactly the class of
134		// misconfiguration this story exists to catch) would otherwise pass
135		// this check and only fail later, whenever S7's signing code first
136		// tries to read it — deferring the failure this check exists to make
137		// immediate (Oren Castellan, PR #18 review).
138		if fi.IsDir() {
139			return "", fmt.Errorf("config: APP_MODE=issuer set but JWT_SIGNING_KEY_FILE %q is a directory, not a file", path)
140		}
141		f, err := os.Open(path)
142		if err != nil {
143			return "", fmt.Errorf("config: APP_MODE=issuer set but JWT_SIGNING_KEY_FILE %q is not readable: %w", path, err)
144		}
145		defer func() { _ = f.Close() }()
146		// Read one byte rather than open-close: an empty file (a Secret
147		// mounted but never populated) opens without error and would
148		// otherwise pass silently.
149		var buf [1]byte
150		if _, err := f.Read(buf[:]); err != nil {
151			return "", fmt.Errorf("config: APP_MODE=issuer set but JWT_SIGNING_KEY_FILE %q is empty or unreadable: %w", path, err)
152		}
153		return path, nil
154	}
155	
156	func resolveDSN() (string, error) {
157		if path := os.Getenv("DB_DSN_FILE"); path != "" {
158			b, err := os.ReadFile(path)
159			if err != nil {
160				return "", fmt.Errorf("config: reading DB_DSN_FILE %q: %w", path, err)
161			}
162			return strings.TrimSpace(string(b)), nil
163		}
164		return os.Getenv("DB_DSN"), nil
165	}

Now building the failed-grant rate limiter component for the issuer endpoint (criterion 5) — ready for LT-51 to wire in.
Write /Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID-worktrees/LT-40/internal/api/grantlimiter.go
input
{
  "file_path": "/Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID-worktrees/LT-40/internal/api/grantlimiter.go",
  "content": "package api\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"sync\"\n\t\"time\"\n)\n\n// GrantLimiter enforces the token-issuing endpoint's own failed-grant\n// rate limiting and progressive backoff — handoff-03-auth.md v3:\n// \"Token-endpoint brute-force protection, separate from the resource-API\n// rate limits ... failed-grant rate limiting and progressive backoff per\n// client_id and per source IP on the token-issuing endpoint itself, plus\n// alerting on a sustained run of failed grants against one client_id.\"\n//\n// This is genuinely separate from RateLimiter (ratelimit.go): that type\n// gates the resource API by an already-authenticated sub, at a fixed\n// per-minute rate; this one gates the endpoint that issues the\n// credential in the first place, keyed independently by client_id AND\n// by source IP — a distributed brute force (many IPs, one client_id)\n// and a single-source credential-stuffing run (one IP, many client_ids)\n// are both attack shapes this is meant to slow down, and either key\n// exceeding its own backoff blocks the attempt.\n//\n// Not wired to a live endpoint by this story: /auth/token's real grant\n// logic is LT-51's scope (this story's non-goals). This is the\n// ready-to-use component LT-51 constructs and calls from the real\n// handler — building it now, tested in isolation, means LT-51 doesn't\n// also have to design a backoff/alerting scheme under its own deadline.\ntype GrantLimiter interface {\n\t// Allow reports whether a grant attempt for clientID from sourceIP\n\t// may proceed right now — false while either key is in backoff.\n\tAllow(ctx context.Context, clientID, sourceIP string) (bool, error)\n\t// RecordFailure records a failed grant attempt for both clientID and\n\t// sourceIP, advancing each key's backoff state independently.\n\t// Crossing the alert threshold for a given client_id (not source IP\n\t// — the requirement names client_id specifically) fires alert\n\t// exactly once per crossing, not once per subsequent failure.\n\tRecordFailure(ctx context.Context, clientID, sourceIP string)\n\t// RecordSuccess clears backoff state for both clientID and\n\t// sourceIP — a successful grant resets the count, standard\n\t// exponential-backoff practice: this deters sustained brute force,\n\t// it does not permanently degrade a legitimate client that mistyped\n\t// a secret once.\n\tRecordSuccess(ctx context.Context, clientID, sourceIP string)\n}\n\n// grantBackoffState is one key's (a client_id's, or a source IP's)\n// consecutive-failure bookkeeping.\ntype grantBackoffState struct {\n\tconsecutiveFailures int\n\tblockedUntil        time.Time\n\talerted             bool\n}\n\n// InProcessGrantLimiter is an in-process implementation. As with\n// RateLimiter/TouchCounter's in-process implementations, a shared store\n// is the multi-instance replacement behind the same interface — the\n// mechanism is this track's call, per handoff-03-auth.md v3.\ntype InProcessGrantLimiter struct {\n\tmu             sync.Mutex\n\tbyClientID     map[string]*grantBackoffState\n\tbySourceIP     map[string]*grantBackoffState\n\tbaseBackoff    time.Duration\n\tmaxBackoff     time.Duration\n\talertThreshold int\n\talertFn        func(clientID string, consecutiveFailures int)\n\tnow            func() time.Time\n}\n\n// NewInProcessGrantLimiter constructs a limiter with exponential backoff\n// starting at baseBackoff (doubling per consecutive failure, capped at\n// maxBackoff) and alertFn called once per client_id crossing\n// alertThreshold consecutive failures. alertFn may be nil (no alerting —\n// tests use this; production wiring passes a real sink).\nfunc NewInProcessGrantLimiter(baseBackoff, maxBackoff time.Duration, alertThreshold int, alertFn func(clientID string, consecutiveFailures int)) *InProcessGrantLimiter {\n\treturn &InProcessGrantLimiter{\n\t\tbyClientID:     make(map[string]*grantBackoffState),\n\t\tbySourceIP:     make(map[string]*grantBackoffState),\n\t\tbaseBackoff:    baseBackoff,\n\t\tmaxBackoff:     maxBackoff,\n\t\talertThreshold: alertThreshold,\n\t\talertFn:        alertFn,\n\t\tnow:            time.Now,\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc (l *InProcessGrantLimiter) Allow(_ context.Context, clientID, sourceIP string) (bool, error) {\n\tl.mu.Lock()\n\tdefer l.mu.Unlock()\n\tnow := l.now()\n\treturn !isBlocked(l.byClientID[clientID], now) && !isBlocked(l.bySourceIP[sourceIP], now), nil\n}\n\nfunc isBlocked(s *grantBackoffState, now time.Time) bool {\n\treturn s != nil && now.Before(s.blockedUntil)\n}\n\nfunc (l *InProcessGrantLimiter) RecordFailure(_ context.Context, clientID, sourceIP string) {\n\tl.mu.Lock()\n\tdefer l.mu.Unlock()\n\tnow := l.now()\n\n\tcs := l.advance(l.byClientID, clientID, now)\n\tl.advance(l.bySourceIP, sourceIP, now)\n\n\tif cs.consecutiveFailures >= l.alertThreshold && !cs.alerted {\n\t\tcs.alerted = true\n\t\tif l.alertFn != nil {\n\t\t\tl.alertFn(clientID, cs.consecutiveFailures)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n}\n\n// advance increments key's consecutive-failure count and sets its\n// exponential backoff window, creating the state entry if this is its\n// first failure.\nfunc (l *InProcessGrantLimiter) advance(m map[string]*grantBackoffState, key string, now time.Time) *grantBackoffState {\n\ts, ok := m[key]\n\tif !ok {\n\t\ts = &grantBackoffState{}\n\t\tm[key] = s\n\t}\n\ts.consecutiveFailures++\n\tbackoff := l.baseBackoff << uint(s.consecutiveFailures-1) // 2^(n-1) * base\n\tif backoff > l.maxBackoff || backoff <= 0 {                // overflow guard: a left-shifted duration can wrap negative\n\t\tbackoff = l.maxBackoff\n\t}\n\ts.blockedUntil = now.Add(backoff)\n\treturn s\n}\n\nfunc (l *InProcessGrantLimiter) RecordSuccess(_ context.Context, clientID, sourceIP string) {\n\tl.mu.Lock()\n\tdefer l.mu.Unlock()\n\tdelete(l.byClientID, clientID)\n\tdelete(l.bySourceIP, sourceIP)\n}\n"
}

Advance 1.5s — should be allowed again.\n\tfakeNow = fakeNow.Add(1500 * time.Millisecond)\n\tif allow, _ := l.Allow(ctx, \"client-a\", \"«IP»\"); !allow {\n\t\tt.Fatal(\"should be allowed again after the first (1s) backoff elapses\")\n\t}\n\n\tl.RecordFailure(ctx, \"client-a\", \"«IP»\")\n\t// Second consecutive failure: 2s backoff. Advance only 1.5s again —\n\t// should still be blocked, proving the window grew.\n\tfakeNow = fakeNow.Add(1500 * time.Millisecond)\n\tif allow, _ := l.Allow(ctx, \"client-a\", \"«IP»\"); allow {\n\t\tt.Error(\"should still be blocked — the second failure's backoff (2s) hasn't elapsed yet\")\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc TestGrantLimiter_BackoffCappedAtMax(t *testing.T) {\n\tl := NewInProcessGrantLimiter(time.Second, 5*time.Second, 100, nil)\n\tfakeNow := time.Now()\n\tl.now = func() time.Time { return fakeNow }\n\tctx := context.Background()\n\n\tfor i := 0; i < 10; i++ {\n\t\tl.RecordFailure(ctx, \"client-a\", \"«IP»\")\n\t}\n\t// Even after many failures, the backoff must not exceed maxBackoff.\n\tfakeNow = fakeNow.Add(5*time.Second + time.Millisecond)\n\tif allow, _ := l.Allow(ctx, \"client-a\", \"«IP»\"); !allow {\n\t\tt.Error(\"backoff should be capped at maxBackoff (5s), not grow unbounded across 10 failures\")\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc TestGrantLimiter_KeyedIndependentlyByClientIDAndSourceIP(t *testing.T) {\n\tl := NewInProcessGrantLimiter(time.Hour, time.Hour, 100, nil)\n\tctx := context.Background()\n\n\t// A distributed brute force: many source IPs against one client_id.\n\t// The client_id key alone must block a request even from a fresh IP.\n\tl.RecordFailure(ctx, \"client-a\", \"«IP»\")\n\tif allow, _ := l.Allow(ctx, \"client-a\", \"«IP»\"); allow {\n\t\tt.Error(\"a blocked client_id must block attempts from a different source IP too\")\n\t}\n\n\t// A single-source credential-stuffing run: many client_ids from one\n\t// IP. The source-IP key alone must block a fresh client_id from that\n\t// same IP.\n\tif allow, _ := l.Allow(ctx, \"client-b\", \"«IP»\"); allow {\n\t\tt.Error(\"a blocked source IP must block attempts for a different client_id too\")\n\t}\n\n\t// A genuinely unrelated (client_id, IP) pair must not be affected.\n\tif allow, _ := l.Allow(ctx, \"client-c\", \"«IP»\"); !allow {\n\t\tt.Error(\"an unrelated client_id/source IP pair must not be blocked\")\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc TestGrantLimiter_SuccessResetsBackoff(t *testing.T) {\n\tl := NewInProcessGrantLimiter(time.Hour, time.Hour, 100, nil)\n\tctx := context.Background()\n\n\tl.RecordFailure(ctx, \"client-a\", \"«IP»\")\n\tif allow, _ := l.Allow(ctx, \"client-a\", \"«IP»\"); allow {\n\t\tt.Fatal(\"should be blocked after a failure\")\n\t}\n\n\tl.RecordSuccess(ctx, \"client-a\", \"«IP»\")\n\tif allow, _ := l.Allow(ctx, \"client-a\", \"«IP»\"); !allow {\n\t\tt.Error(\"a successful grant should reset backoff state entirely\")\n\t}\n}\n\n// TestGrantLimiter_AlertsOnceOnSustainedFailureRun is\n// handoff-03-auth.md v3's \"alerting on a sustained run of failed grants\n// against one client_id\" — fired exactly once per threshold crossing,\n// not once per failure past it (which would just be alert-fatigue noise\n// indistinguishable from the failures themselves).\nfunc TestGrantLimiter_AlertsOnceOnSustainedFailureRun(t *testing.T) {\n\tvar alerts []int\n\tl := NewInProcessGrantLimiter(time.Nanosecond, time.Nanosecond, 3, func(clientID string, consecutiveFailures int) {\n\t\talerts = append(alerts, consecutiveFailures)\n\t})\n\tfakeNow := time.Now()\n\tl.now = func() time.Time { return fakeNow }\n\tctx := context.Background()\n\n\tfor i := 0; i < 6; i++ {\n\t\tl.RecordFailure(ctx, \"client-a\", \"«IP»\")\n\t\tfakeNow = fakeNow.Add(time.Millisecond) // clear the (nanosecond) backoff each time\n\t}\n\n\tif len(alerts) != 1 {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"alerts fired = %v, want exactly one (at the threshold crossing)\", alerts)\n\t}\n\tif alerts[0] != 3 {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"alert fired at %d consecutive failures, want at the threshold (3)\", alerts[0])\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc TestGrantLimiter_AlertOnlyNamesClientID_NotSourceIP(t *testing.T) {\n\t// The requirement names client_id specifically for alerting — a\n\t// source IP hitting the threshold across many different client_ids\n\t// (each individually under threshold) is a different signal\n\t// (distributed credential stuffing) this limiter still blocks via\n\t// the source-IP key, but doesn't claim to alert on by itself.\n\tvar alerted bool\n\tl := NewInProcessGrantLimiter(time.Nanosecond, time.Nanosecond, 3, func(clientID string, n int) {\n\t\talerted = true\n\t})\n\tfakeNow := time.Now()\n\tl.now = func() time.Time { return fakeNow }\n\tctx := context.Background()\n\n\tfor i := 0; package dao

import "fmt"

// New selects a backend implementation by driver name, matching
// multi-db-strategy.md §1's factory signature exactly. "postgres" and
// "cockroachdb" are distinct driver strings rather than one because the
// engine-aware retry seam (§4) needs to know which engine it is talking to.
//
// No backend is registered yet — S3 (postgres/cockroachdb) and S4 (sqlite)
// register themselves via Register in their own package init(). Until
// then, New always returns an error for every driver name, which is
// expected: this story's non-goal is real I/O, not a callable factory.
func New(driver, dsn string) (Repository, error) {
	ctor, ok := drivers[driver]
	if !ok {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("dao: unknown driver %q", driver)
	}
	return ctor(dsn)
}

type driverCtor func(dsn string) (Repository, error)

var drivers = map[string]driverCtor{}

// Register is called by a backend package's init() to register itself
// under a driver name (e.g. "postgres", "cockroachdb", "sqlite"). Not part
// of 05's Go-shaped contract — an internal wiring mechanism this track owns
// so New's signature never has to change as backends are added.
func Register(name string, ctor driverCtor) {
	drivers[name] = ctor
}

1	// jwks.go implements a minimal RFC 7517 JSON Web Key Set client: fetch,
2	// parse RSA public keys, cache with a short TTL. handoff-03-auth.md v4:
3	// "Public-key distribution for the verifier ... JWKS served by the issuer
4	// Deployment, reachable only in-cluster via its own ClusterIP Service ...
5	// Verifier caches the fetched key set in memory with a short TTL (matching
6	// token TTL is reasonable) to avoid a JWKS fetch on every single
7	// verification."
8	package api
9	
10	import (
11		"crypto/rsa"
12		"encoding/base64"
13		"encoding/json"
14		"fmt"
15		"io"
16		"math/big"
17		"net/http"
18		"sync"
19		"time"
20	)
21	
22	// jwk is one entry in a JWKS response — only the fields this verifier
23	// needs to reconstruct an RSA public key (RFC 7517 §4, RFC 7518 §6.3.1).
24	// Fields this verifier doesn't use (x5c, x5t, ...) are simply not
25	// unmarshaled; unknown fields in the source JSON are not an error.
26	type jwk struct {
27		Kty string `json:"kty"`
28		Kid string `json:"kid"`
29		Alg string `json:"alg"`
30		Use string `json:"use"`
31		N   string `json:"n"` // base64url, unpadded — the RSA modulus
32		E   string `json:"e"` // base64url, unpadded — the RSA public exponent
33	}
34	
35	type jwksResponse struct {
36		Keys []jwk `json:"keys"`
37	}
38	
39	// parseJWKS decodes a JWKS JSON document into a map of kid -> public key.
40	// Any key with kty != "RSA" is skipped (not an error) — a future non-RSA
41	// key in the set (e.g. during an algorithm migration) shouldn't break
42	// verification of tokens signed with the RSA keys that ARE present.
43	func parseJWKS(data []byte) (map[string]*rsa.PublicKey, error) {
44		var resp jwksResponse
45		if err := json.Unmarshal(data, &resp); err != nil {
46			return nil, fmt.Errorf("api: parsing JWKS: %w", err)
47		}
48		keys := make(map[string]*rsa.PublicKey, len(resp.Keys))
49		for _, k := range resp.Keys {
50			if k.Kty != "RSA" {
51				continue
52			}
53			if k.Kid == "" {
54				return nil, fmt.Errorf("api: JWKS RSA key missing kid")
55			}
56			pub, err := rsaPublicKeyFromJWK(k)
57			if err != nil {
58				return nil, fmt.Errorf("api: JWKS key %q: %w", k.Kid, err)
59			}
60			keys[k.Kid] = pub
61		}
62		return keys, nil
63	}
64	
65	func rsaPublicKeyFromJWK(k jwk) (*rsa.PublicKey, error) {
66		nBytes, err := base64.RawURLEncoding.DecodeString(k.N)
67		if err != nil {
68			return nil, fmt.Errorf("decoding n: %w", err)
69		}
70		eBytes, err := base64.RawURLEncoding.DecodeString(k.E)
71		if err != nil {
72			return nil, fmt.Errorf("decoding e: %w", err)
73		}
74		n := new(big.Int).SetBytes(nBytes)
75		e := new(big.Int).SetBytes(eBytes)
76		if !e.IsInt64() {
77			return nil, fmt.Errorf("exponent out of range")
78		}
79		return &rsa.PublicKey{N: n, E: int(e.Int64())}, nil
80	}
81	
82	// KeySource resolves a kid to the RSA public key that should verify a
83	// token carrying it. JWKSCache is the real implementation; tests use a
84	// map-backed fake.
85	type KeySource interface {
86		KeyForKid(kid string) (*rsa.PublicKey, error)
87	}
88	
89	// JWKSCache fetches a JWKS document from url and caches the parsed key
90	// set for ttl, refetching only after it expires — "to avoid a JWKS fetch
91	// on every single verification" (handoff-03-auth.md v4). Supports at
92	// least two concurrently valid kids during a rotation overlap window by
93	// construction: it caches the WHOLE key set the issuer publishes, not
94	// just the most recent key, so an old and a new key both verify
95	// successfully for as long as the issuer's own JWKS response lists both.
96	type JWKSCache struct {
97		url        string
98		ttl        time.Duration
99		httpClient *http.Client
100	
101		mu        sync.Mutex
102		keys      map[string]*rsa.PublicKey
103		fetchedAt time.Time
104	}
105	
106	// NewJWKSCache constructs a cache fetching from url, refetching at most
107	// once per ttl. A nil httpClient uses http.DefaultClient.
108	func NewJWKSCache(url string, ttl time.Duration, httpClient *http.Client) *JWKSCache {
109		if httpClient == nil {
110			httpClient = http.DefaultClient
111		}
112		return &JWKSCache{url: url, ttl: ttl, httpClient: httpClient}
113	}
114	
115	func (c *JWKSCache) KeyForKid(kid string) (*rsa.PublicKey, error) {
116		c.mu.Lock()
117		defer c.mu.Unlock()
118	
119		if c.keys == nil || time.Since(c.fetchedAt) >= c.ttl {
120			keys, err := c.fetch()
121			if err != nil {
122				// A stale-but-present cache is preferable to a hard failure
123				// on every request the moment one refresh fails (a
124				// transient network blip to the issuer's in-cluster
125				// Service) — verification with a stale key set still
126				// correctly rejects a token signed by a kid that's been
127				// fully retired and removed from the real set, it just
128				// risks accepting a very recently rotated-in key slightly
129				// late, which is not a security regression (the overlap
130				// window already tolerates both old and new keys).
131				if c.keys == nil {
132					return nil, err
133				}
134			} else {
135				c.keys = keys
136				c.fetchedAt = time.Now()
137			}
138		}
139	
140		key, ok := c.keys[kid]
141		if !ok {
142			return nil, fmt.Errorf("api: no key found for kid %q", kid)
143		}
144		return key, nil
145	}
146	
147	func (c *JWKSCache) fetch() (map[string]*rsa.PublicKey, error) {
148		resp, err := c.httpClient.Get(c.url)
149		if err != nil {
150			return nil, fmt.Errorf("api: fetching JWKS from %s: %w", c.url, err)
151		}
152		defer func() { _ = resp.Body.Close() }()
153		if resp.StatusCode != http.StatusOK {
154			return nil, fmt.Errorf("api: fetching JWKS from %s: status %d", c.url, resp.StatusCode)
155		}
156		// 1MiB ceiling — a JWKS response is a handful of RSA keys, never
157		// this large; capped so a misbehaving/compromised issuer endpoint
158		// can't force this process to buffer an unbounded response body.
159		body, err := io.ReadAll(io.LimitReader(resp.Body, 1<<20))
160		if err != nil {
161			return nil, fmt.Errorf("api: reading JWKS response: %w", err)
162		}
163		return parseJWKS(body)
164	}

55	// specific reason (expired, wrong audience, bad signature, unknown kid,
56	// disallowed algorithm) is never distinguishable to the caller of this
57	// function beyond "verification failed," which is what makes
58	// writeUnauthorized's single, undifferentiated 401 body correct here
59	// without an extra translation step.
60	func (v *jwtVerifier) verify(tokenString string) (AuthContext, error) {
61		var c claims
62		token, err := jwt.ParseWithClaims(tokenString, &c, func(t *jwt.Token) (any, error) {
63			kid, _ := t.Header["kid"].(string)
64			if kid == "" {
65				return nil, errors.New("token header has no kid")
66			}
67			return v.keys.KeyForKid(kid)
68		}, jwt.WithValidMethods([]string{jwtAlgorithm}), jwt.WithIssuer(v.issuer))
69		if err != nil {
70			return AuthContext{}, err
71		}
72		if !token.Valid {
73			return AuthContext{}, errors.New("token invalid")
74		}
75	
76		// Audience checked explicitly, not via jwt.WithAudience above: v5's
77		// audience option treats an all-nil/absent Audience claim
78		// inconsistently across versions in a way we don't want to depend
79		// on — asserting it here directly means this check's behavior is
80		// this file's own to guarantee, not the library's default to trust.
81		if !hasAudience(c.RegisteredClaims.Audience, v.audience) {
82			return AuthContext{}, errors.New("token audience mismatch")
83		}

42	// key in the set (e.g. during an algorithm migration) shouldn't break
43	// verification of tokens signed with the RSA keys that ARE present.
44	func parseJWKS(data []byte) (map[string]*rsa.PublicKey, error) {
45		var resp jwksResponse
46		if err := json.Unmarshal(data, &resp); err != nil {
47			return nil, fmt.Errorf("api: parsing JWKS: %w", err)
48		}
49		keys := make(map[string]*rsa.PublicKey, len(resp.Keys))
50		for _, k := range resp.Keys {
51			if k.Kty != "RSA" {
52				continue
53			}
54			if k.Kid == "" {
55				return nil, fmt.Errorf("api: JWKS RSA key missing kid")
56			}
57			pub, err := rsaPublicKeyFromJWK(k)
58			if err != nil {
59				return nil, fmt.Errorf("api: JWKS key %q: %w", k.Kid, err)
60			}
61			keys[k.Kid] = pub
62		}
63		return keys, nil
64	}
65	
66	func rsaPublicKeyFromJWK(k jwk) (*rsa.PublicKey, error) {
67		nBytes, err := base64.RawURLEncoding.DecodeString(k.N)
68		if err != nil {
69			return nil, fmt.Errorf("decoding n: %w", err)
70		}
71		eBytes, err := base64.RawURLEncoding.DecodeString(k.E)
72		if err != nil {
73			return nil, fmt.Errorf("decoding e: %w", err)
74		}
75		n := new(big.Int).SetBytes(nBytes)
76		e := new(big.Int).SetBytes(eBytes)
77		if !e.IsInt64() {
78			return nil, fmt.Errorf("exponent out of range")
79		}
80		return &rsa.PublicKey{N: n, E: int(e.Int64())}, nil
81	}
82	
83	// KeySource resolves a kid to the RSA public key that should verify a
84	// token carrying it. JWKSCache is the real implementation; tests use a
85	// map-backed fake. ctx carries the calling request's own deadline —
86	// KeyForKid's real implementation must bound its network I/O by it, not
87	// by some separate, undocumented timeout (Nolan Reyes, PR #30 review: a
88	// hung fetch that ignores the caller's context defeats the deadline
89	// middleware's whole guarantee).
90	type KeySource interface {
91		KeyForKid(ctx context.Context, kid string) (*rsa.PublicKey, error)
92	}
93	
94	// JWKSCache fetches a JWKS document from url and caches the parsed key
95	// set for ttl, refetching only after it expires — "to avoid a JWKS fetch
96	// on every single verification" (handoff-03-auth.md v4). Supports at
97	// least two concurrently valid kids during a rotation overlap window by
98	// construction: it caches the WHOLE key set the issuer publishes, not
99	// just the most recent key, so an old and a new key both verify
100	// successfully for as long as the issuer's own JWKS response lists both.
101	type JWKSCache struct {
102		url        string
103		ttl        time.Duration
104		httpClient *http.Client
105	
106		mu        sync.Mutex
107		keys      map[string]*rsa.PublicKey
108		fetchedAt time.Time
109	}
110	
111	// defaultJWKSClientTimeout backstops a hung fetch (a connection that
112	// accepts but never responds — not a hard refusal, which fails fast on
113	// its own) when no context deadline is shorter. Distinct from and
114	// independent of a per-request context deadline: this fires even for a
115	// call that somehow reaches fetch() with no deadline on its context at
116	// all, which the http.Client.Timeout field guards against regardless of
117	// context (Nolan Reyes, PR #30 review — the shipped wiring passed nil
118	// for httpClient, resolving to http.DefaultClient, whose zero-value
119	// Timeout means "none").
120	const defaultJWKSClientTimeout = 10 * time.Second
121	
122	// NewJWKSCache constructs a cache fetching from url, refetching at most
123	// once per ttl. A nil httpClient constructs one with
124	// defaultJWKSClientTimeout — never http.DefaultClient, whose Timeout is
125	// unset (no bound at all) by default.
126	func NewJWKSCache(url string, ttl time.Duration, httpClient *http.Client) *JWKSCache {
127		if httpClient == nil {
128			httpClient = &http.Client{Timeout: defaultJWKSClientTimeout}
129		}
130		return &JWKSCache{url: url, ttl: ttl, httpClient: httpClient}
131	}
132	
133	// KeyForKid never holds c.mu across the network fetch (Nolan Reyes, PR
134	// #30 review, finding #2): the prior shape held the lock for the whole
135	// function body, so one hung fetch stalled every other concurrent
136	// request through this same cache instance — i.e. every authenticated
137	// request the verifying Deployment was serving, not just the one that
138	// triggered the refresh. Concurrent callers that both decide a refresh
139	// is due may both fetch; that's cheap, occasional duplicate work, and
140	// far preferable to serializing the whole authenticated surface behind
141	// one goroutine's network call.
142	func (c *JWKSCache) KeyForKid(ctx context.Context, kid string) (*rsa.PublicKey, error) {
143		c.mu.Lock()
144		needsRefresh := c.keys == nil || time.Since(c.fetchedAt) >= c.ttl
145		hadCache := c.keys != nil
146		c.mu.Unlock()
147	
148		if needsRefresh {
149			fresh, err := c.fetch(ctx)
150			switch {
151			case err == nil:
152				c.mu.Lock()
153				c.keys = fresh
154				c.fetchedAt = time.Now()
155				c.mu.Unlock()
156			case !hadCache:
157				// No stale cache to fall back on — this is the cold-start
158				// case (or a class of kid the issuer has never published):
159				// fail this call, bounded by ctx/the client timeout above,
160				// rather than hang.
161				return nil, err
162			default:
163				// A stale-but-present cache is preferable to a hard failure
164				// on every request the moment one refresh fails (a
165				// transient network blip to the issuer's in-cluster
166				// Service) — verification with a stale key set still
167				// correctly rejects a token signed by a kid that's been
168				// fully retired and removed from the real set, it just
169				// risks accepting a very recently rotated-in key slightly
170				// late, which is not a security regression (the overlap
171				// window already tolerates both old and new keys).
172			}
173		}
174	
175		c.mu.Lock()
176		keys := c.keys
177		c.mu.Unlock()
178		key, ok := keys[kid]
179		if !ok {
180			return nil, fmt.Errorf("api: no key found for kid %q", kid)
181		}
182		return key, nil
183	}
184	
185	func (c *JWKSCache) fetch(ctx context.Context) (map[string]*rsa.PublicKey, error) {
186		req, err := http.NewRequestWithContext(ctx, http.MethodGet, c.url, nil)
187		if err != nil {
188			return nil, fmt.Errorf("api: building JWKS request for %s: %w", c.url, err)
189		}
190		resp, err := c.httpClient.Do(req)
191		if err != nil {
192			return nil, fmt.Errorf("api: fetching JWKS from %s: %w", c.url, err)
193		}
194		defer func() { _ = resp.Body.Close() }()
195		if resp.StatusCode != http.StatusOK {
196			return nil, fmt.Errorf("api: fetching JWKS from %s: status %d", c.url, resp.StatusCode)
197		}
198		// 1MiB ceiling — a JWKS response is a handful of RSA keys, never
199		// this large; capped so a misbehaving/compromised issuer endpoint
200		// can't force this process to buffer an unbounded response body.
201		body, err := io.ReadAll(io.LimitReader(resp.Body, 1<<20))
202		if err != nil {
203			return nil, fmt.Errorf("api: reading JWKS response: %w", err)
204		}
205		return parseJWKS(body)
206	}

Bounding\n//     staleness at a multiple of the normal refresh interval (ttl) turns\n//     \"fail open forever\" into \"fail open for a bounded window, then\n//     fail closed\" — resilient to a brief blip, not to an extended one.\n//   - an unknown kid could be a key the issuer rotated in since the last\n//     TTL-driven refresh; without an out-of-band refetch, a freshly\n//     rotated key would be rejected for up to a full TTL. Rate-limited\n//     so an unknown-kid flood can't be used to hammer the issuer's JWKS\n//     endpoint.\nconst (\n\tjwksMaxStalenessMultiple      = 3\n\tjwksUnknownKidRefetchInterval = 30 * time.Second\n)\n\n// JWKSCache fetches a JWKS document from url and caches the parsed key\n// set for ttl, refetching only after it expires — \"to avoid a JWKS fetch\n// on every single verification\" (handoff-03-auth.md v4). Supports at\n// least two concurrently valid kids during a rotation overlap window by\n// construction: it caches the WHOLE key set the issuer publishes, not\n// just the most recent key, so an old and a new key both verify\n// successfully for as long as the issuer's own JWKS response lists both.\ntype JWKSCache struct {\n\turl          string\n\tttl          time.Duration\n\tmaxStaleness time.Duration\n\thttpClient   *http.Client\n\tnow          func() time.Time\n\n\tmu                  sync.Mutex\n\tkeys                map[string]*rsa.PublicKey\n\tfetchedAt           time.Time // last SUCCESSFUL fetch\n\tconsecutiveFailures int\n\tstaleAlerted        bool\n\tlastUnknownKidRetry time.Time\n}\n\n// defaultJWKSClientTimeout backstops a hung fetch (a connection that\n// accepts but never responds — not a hard refusal, which fails fast on\n// its own) when no context deadline is shorter. Distinct from and\n// independent of a per-request context deadline: this fires even for a\n// call that somehow reaches fetch() with no deadline on its context at\n// all, which the http.Client.Timeout field guards against regardless of\n// context (Nolan Reyes, PR #30 review — the shipped wiring passed nil\n// for httpClient, resolving to http.DefaultClient, whose zero-value\n// Timeout means \"none\").\nconst defaultJWKSClientTimeout = 10 * time.Second\n\n// NewJWKSCache constructs a cache fetching from url, refetching at most\n// once per ttl (and, for an unrecognized kid specifically, at most once\n// per jwksUnknownKidRefetchInterval outside the normal TTL cycle). Concurrent callers that both decide a refresh\n// is due may both fetch; that's cheap, occasional duplicate work, and far\n// preferable to serializing the whole authenticated surface behind one\n// goroutine's network call.\nfunc (c *JWKSCache) KeyForKid(ctx context.Context, kid string) (*rsa.PublicKey, error) {\n\tc.mu.Lock()\n\tneedsRefresh := c.keys == nil || c.now().Sub(c.fetchedAt) >= c.ttl\n\tc.mu.Unlock()\n\n\tif needsRefresh {\n\t\tif err := c.refresh(ctx); err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, err\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tif key, ok := c.lookupKey(kid); ok {\n\t\treturn key, nil\n\t}\n\n\t// Unknown kid: might be a key the issuer rotated in since the last\n\t// TTL-driven refresh. One rate-limited out-of-band refetch before\n\t// giving up, so a freshly-rotated key isn't rejected for up to a\n\t// full TTL (Tomasz Wrede, 02 cold review). Best-effort: its error is\n\t// deliberately ignored here — the outcome that matters is whether\n\t// the retry populated the kid we're actually looking for, and a\n\t// refresh() failure here has already been handled (logged / staleness\n\t// tracked) inside refresh() itself.\n\tif c.reserveUnknownKidRetry() {\n\t\t_ = c.refresh(ctx)\n\t\tif key, ok := c.lookupKey(kid); ok {\n\t\t\treturn key, nil\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"api: no key found for kid %q\", kid)\n}\n\nfunc (c *JWKSCache) lookupKey(kid string) (*rsa.PublicKey, bool) {\n\tc.mu.Lock()\n\tdefer c.mu.Unlock()\n\tk, ok := c.keys[kid]\n\treturn k, ok\n}\n\n// reserveUnknownKidRetry reports whether an unknown-kid-triggered refetch\n// may proceed now, and if so atomically marks one as just having started\n// — a caller enumerating unknown kids can trigger at most one extra\n// fetch per jwksUnknownKidRefetchInterval, not one per request.\nfunc (c *JWKSCache) reserveUnknownKidRetry() bool {\n\tc.mu.Lock()\n\tdefer c.mu.Unlock()\n\tif c.now().Sub(c.lastUnknownKidRetry) < jwksUnknownKidRefetchInterval {\n\t\treturn false\n\t}\n\tc.lastUnknownKidRetry = c.now()\n\treturn true\n}\n\n// refresh performs the network fetch (no lock held during it) and\n// updates cache state. Returns an error only when the caller must fail\n// the whole verification: no cache exists yet (cold start against an\n// unreachable issuer), or the existing cache has exceeded maxStaleness —\n// wrapped in ErrJWKSStale in the latter case specifically, so the\n// middleware can log AuditJWKSUnavailable rather than an ordinary auth\n// failure. A transient failure within the staleness bound returns nil:\n// the stale cache is still good enough to use, per the resilience this\n// mechanism exists for.\nfunc (c *JWKSCache) refresh(ctx context.Context) error {\n\tfresh, err := c.fetch(ctx)\n\n\tc.mu.Lock()\n\tdefer c.mu.Unlock()\n\n\tif err == nil {\n\t\tc.keys = fresh\n\t\tc.fetchedAt = c.now()\n\t\tc.consecutiveFailures = 0\n\t\tc.staleAlerted = false\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\n\tc.consecutiveFailures++\n\n\tif c.keys == nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\tstaleSince := c.now().Sub(c.fetchedAt)\n\tif staleSince > c.maxStaleness {\n\t\tif !c.staleAlerted {\n\t\t\tc.staleAlerted = true\n\t\t\t// Log line for 04's alerting pipeline on a prolonged\n\t\t\t// fetch-failure run — not a policy decision (AuditLogger is\n\t\t\t// for those), an operational signal that this process's own\n\t\t\t// key infrastructure is degraded.\n\t\t\tlog.Printf(\"api: JWKS unrefreshable for %s across %d consecutive failures (exceeds max staleness %s) — failing closed: %v\",\n\t\t\t\tstaleSince, c.consecutiveFailures, c.maxStaleness, err)\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"%w: stale for %s across %d consecutive failures: %v\", ErrJWKSStale, staleSince, c.consecutiveFailures, err)\n\t}\n\treturn nil\n}\n\nfunc (c *JWKSCache) fetch(ctx context.Context) (map[string]*rsa.PublicKey, error) {\n\treq, err := http.NewRequestWithContext(ctx, http.MethodGet, c.url, nil)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"api: building JWKS request for %s: %w\", c.url, err)\n\t}\n\tresp, err := c.httpClient.Do(req)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"api: fetching JWKS from %s: %w\", c.url, err)\n\t}\n\tdefer func() { _ = resp.Body.Close() }()\n\tif resp.StatusCode != http.StatusOK {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"api: fetching JWKS from %s: status %d\", c.url, resp.StatusCode)\n\t}\n\t// 1MiB ceiling — a JWKS response is a handful of RSA keys, never\n\t// this large; capped so a misbehaving/compromised issuer endpoint\n\t// can't force this process to buffer an unbounded response body.\n\tbody, err := io.ReadAll(io.LimitReader(resp.Body, 1<<20))\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"api: reading JWKS response: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\treturn parseJWKS(body)\n}\n"

  "file_path": "/Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID-worktrees/LT-40/internal/api/jwtmiddleware.go"

result
1	// jwtmiddleware.go is LT-40's core: verify the client-credentials JWT
2	// scheme from handoff-03-auth.md v3/v4 and populate AuthContext for
3	// LT-39's already-built handlers. This is the actual integration seam
4	// with S6's work, not new response-shape design — a verification failure
5	// uses the exact same writeUnauthorized this package's handlers already
6	// call for a missing AuthContext.
7	package api
8	
9	import (
10		"context"
11		"errors"
12		"net/http"
13		"strings"
14		"time"
15	
16		"github.com/golang-jwt/jwt/v5"
17	)
18	
19	// jwtAlgorithm is the one signing algorithm this verifier ever accepts.
20	// Passed explicitly to jwt.WithValidMethods on every parse — never left
21	// to the library's default, per handoff-03-auth.md v3's explicit
22	// instruction ("check your JWT library's default behavior — several
23	// popular libraries have shipped this vulnerability by not doing this by
24	// default") and RFC 8725 §3.1. This is what makes alg:none and an
25	// HMAC-with-the-public-key-as-secret downgrade both fail before the
26	// library ever calls KeySource — jwt/v5's WithValidMethods rejects a
27	// token whose header alg isn't in this allowlist ahead of signature
28	// verification, not as a secondary check after.
29	const jwtAlgorithm = "RS256"
30	
31	// claims is this service's JWT claim set (handoff-03-auth.md v3's
32	// minimum: sub, scope, exp, iat, aud, plus iss and kid via the header).
33	// RegisteredClaims gives exp/iat/iss/aud validation for free from the
34	// library — this verifier still explicitly re-checks Audience below
35	// because jwt/v5's built-in audience check (via WithAudience) does not
36	// distinguish "no aud claim at all" from "aud present but empty," and a
37	// token with no audience claim must not silently pass as if it matched.
38	type claims struct {
39		Scope string `json:"scope"`
40		jwt.RegisteredClaims
41	}
42	
43	// jwtVerifier is the collaborator NewJWTMiddleware wraps — an interface
44	// so a test can supply a fake instead of a real *JWKSCache doing network
45	// I/O, mirroring this package's existing test-double approach
46	// (decisions/test-double-strategy.md).
47	type jwtVerifier struct {
48		keys     KeySource
49		issuer   string
50		audience string
51	}
52	
53	// verify parses and fully validates tokenString, returning the populated
54	// AuthContext on success. Every failure returns a generic error — the
55	// specific reason (expired, wrong audience, bad signature, unknown kid,
56	// disallowed algorithm) is never distinguishable to the caller of this
57	// function beyond "verification failed," which is what makes
58	// writeUnauthorized's single, undifferentiated 401 body correct here
59	// without an extra translation step.
60	func (v *jwtVerifier) verify(ctx context.Context, tokenString string) (AuthContext, error) {
61		var c claims
62		token, err := jwt.ParseWithClaims(tokenString, &c, func(t *jwt.Token) (any, error) {
63			kid, _ := t.Header["kid"].(string)
64			if kid == "" {
65				return nil, errors.New("token header has no kid")
66			}
67			return v.keys.KeyForKid(ctx, kid)
68		}, jwt.WithValidMethods([]string{jwtAlgorithm}), jwt.WithIssuer(v.issuer))
69		if err != nil {
70			return AuthContext{}, err
71		}
72		if !token.Valid {
73			return AuthContext{}, errors.New("token invalid")
74		}
75	
76		// Audience checked explicitly, not via jwt.WithAudience above: v5's
77		// audience option treats an all-nil/absent Audience claim
78		// inconsistently across versions in a way we don't want to depend
79		// on — asserting it here directly means this check's behavior is
80		// this file's own to guarantee, not the library's default to trust.
81		if !hasAudience(c.RegisteredClaims.Audience, v.audience) {
82			return AuthContext{}, errors.New("token audience mismatch")
83		}
84	
85		if c.Subject == "" {
86			return AuthContext{}, errors.New("token has no sub claim")
87		}
88	
89		scope, err := singleScope(c.Scope)
90		if err != nil {
91			return AuthContext{}, err
92		}
93	
94		return AuthContext{Sub: c.Subject, Scope: scope}, nil
95	}
96	
97	func hasAudience(aud jwt.ClaimStrings, want string) bool {
98		for _, a := range aud {
99			if a == want {
100				return true
101			}
102		}
103		return false
104	}
105	
106	// singleScope requires the token's scope claim to name exactly one
107	// recognized scope. handoff-03-auth.md v3 describes the claim as
108	// "space-delimited, values below" without describing how a handler
109	// disambiguates a multi-scope token per request, and AuthContext (LT-39)
110	// carries exactly one Scope by design ("handoff-03-auth.md v3 doesn't
111	// describe multi-scope tokens being disambiguated per-request, so one
112	// request carries exactly one Scope"). Rather than guess which of
113	// several granted scopes a request is "really" using — silently picking
114	// one is exactly the kind of authorization ambiguity this project's
115	// contracts have consistently ruled against — a token naming zero or
116	// more than one recognized scope is rejected outright. This matches the
117	// provisioning model in decisions/search-authz-scoping.md: a client is
118	// provisioned for the one use case its credential exists for.
119	func singleScope(raw string) (Scope, error) {
120		fields := strings.Fields(raw)
121		var found []Scope
122		for _, f := range fields {
123			switch Scope(f) {
124			case ScopeReadOwn, ScopeReadAny, ScopeSearch:
125				found = append(found, Scope(f))
126			}
127		}
128		switch len(found) {
129		case 0:
130			return "", errors.New("token scope claim names no recognized scope")
131		case 1:
132			return found[0], nil
133		default:
134			return "", errors.New("token scope claim names more than one recognized scope, which this verifier does not disambiguate per request")
135		}
136	}
137	
138	// NewJWTMiddleware verifies every request's bearer token and populates
139	// AuthContext on success, per handoff-03-auth.md v3/v4: public-key-only
140	// verification (keys never includes signing-key material — enforced
141	// structurally, since KeySource's real implementation, JWKSCache, only
142	// ever holds *rsa.PublicKey values parsed from a JWKS response), pinned
143	// RS256, kid-based key selection supporting a rotation overlap window (a
144	// KeySource backed by the issuer's full published key set, not a single
145	// cached key), and issuer/audience checks. Verification failure returns
146	// 401 via writeUnauthorized — the same path LT-39's handlers already use
147	// for a missing AuthContext, not a new response shape.
148	func NewJWTMiddleware(keys KeySource, issuer, audience string) func(http.Handler) http.Handler {
149		v := &jwtVerifier{keys: keys, issuer: issuer, audience: audience}
150		return func(next http.Handler) http.Handler {
151			return http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
152				tokenString, ok := bearerToken(r)
153				if !ok {
154					writeUnauthorized(w)
155					return
156				}
157				ac, err := v.verify(tokenString)
158				if err != nil {
159					writeUnauthorized(w)
160					return
161				}
162				r = r.WithContext(WithAuthContext(r.Context(), ac))
163				next.ServeHTTP(w, r)
164			})
165		}
166	}
167	
168	func bearerToken(r *http.Request) (string, bool) {
169		h := r.Header.Get("Authorization")
170		const prefix = "Bearer "
171		if !strings.HasPrefix(h, prefix) {
172			return "", false
173		}
174		tok := strings.TrimSpace(strings.TrimPrefix(h, prefix))
175		if tok == "" {
176			return "", false
177		}
178		return tok, true
179	}
180	
181	// deadlineBudget is the context.WithTimeout duration this middleware
182	// applies to every request before it reaches a handler — the concrete
183	// number decisions/context-propagation.md's ruling required ("this gets
184	// a concrete number attached when [the middleware] is written, not left
185	// as 'some deadline'"). Sized to cover realistic p99 DAO latency
186	// (single-digit milliseconds for an indexed Postgres/CockroachDB point
187	// query or trigram search, per 05-data-ops/research-cockroachdb-postgres-semantics.md)
188	// plus this middleware's own JWKS verification path (a cache hit is
189	// sub-millisecond; a cache-miss fetch to the issuer's in-cluster Service
190	// is bounded by deadlineBudget itself, not a separate timeout, since it
191	// happens inside the same request). 5 seconds leaves an order of
192	// magnitude of headroom over expected p99 without ever handing the DAO
193	// layer a near-zero remaining deadline as a matter of routine operation.
194	const deadlineBudget = 5 * time.Second
195	
196	// NewDeadlineMiddleware applies deadlineBudget to every request's
197	// context before it reaches a handler — the enforcement site
198	// decisions/context-propagation.md's ruling named ("a concrete number
199	// attached when [the middleware] is written"). Composed outermost (see
200	// router wiring), so the budget covers JWT verification (including a
201	// JWKS cache-miss fetch) and the handler's own DAO call under one
202	// deadline, not two independently-sized timeouts that could disagree.
203	func NewDeadlineMiddleware(next http.Handler) http.Handler {
204		return http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
205			ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(r.Context(), deadlineBudget)
206			defer cancel()
207			next.ServeHTTP(w, r.WithContext(ctx))
208		})
209	}

1	package app
2	
3	import (
4		"net/http"
5		"time"
6	
7		"loginid-takehome/internal/api"
8		"loginid-takehome/internal/config"
9		"loginid-takehome/internal/dao"
10	)
11	
12	// jwksCacheTTL matches the token TTL per handoff-03-auth.md v4's own
13	// guidance ("Verifier caches the fetched key set in memory with a short
14	// TTL (matching token TTL is reasonable)") — using the upper bound of the
15	// 5-15 minute TTL range so a cache refresh never fires more often than a
16	// token itself could have already expired and been re-issued.
17	const jwksCacheTTL = 15 * time.Minute
18	
19	// NewVerifierRouter builds api-service's real, authenticated router —
20	// LT-39's handlers (search/retrieve) wired behind LT-40's JWT
21	// verification middleware, plus /healthz. This is the ModeVerifier
22	// Deployment's router; the issuer Deployment (ModeIssuer) still uses the
23	// plain NewRouter above, since it has no need for any of this — it never
24	// verifies inbound tokens, only issues them.
25	//
26	// repo may be nil (e.g. DB_DRIVER unset in a minimal smoke-test
27	// environment); the search/retrieve routes still register in that case,
28	// they'll simply fail at the DAO call with whatever error a nil
29	// Repository produces — this function's job is wiring, not validating
30	// that a database is reachable, which is /healthz's own job (LT-52).
31	func NewVerifierRouter(cfg config.Config, repo dao.Repository) http.Handler {
32		mux := http.NewServeMux()
33		mux.HandleFunc("/healthz", healthHandler("api-service"))
34	
35		deps := api.Deps{
36			Repo:        repo,
37			RateLimiter: api.NewInProcessRateLimiter(),
38			TouchCounter: api.NewInProcessTouchCounter(
39				api.TouchCapPerWindow, api.TouchCapWindow),
40			AuditLog: api.StdoutAuditLogger{},
41			Authz: &api.StopgapAuthorizer{
42				ReadOwnAllow: stopgapReadOwnAllow(cfg),
43			},
44		}
45	
46		keys := api.NewJWKSCache(cfg.AuthJWKSURL, jwksCacheTTL, nil)
47		authMiddleware := api.NewJWTMiddleware(keys, cfg.AuthJWTIssuer, cfg.AuthJWTAudience)
48	
49		// api.NewDeadlineMiddleware outermost: the deadline budget must cover
50		// JWT verification (including a JWKS cache-miss fetch) as well as
51		// the handler's own DAO call, per decisions/context-propagation.md's
52		// ruling — a single deadline, not the middleware's and the handler's
53		// own disagreeing.
54		protect := func(h http.Handler) http.Handler {
55			return api.NewDeadlineMiddleware(authMiddleware(h))
56		}
57	
58		mux.Handle("POST /profiles/search", protect(api.NewSearchHandler(deps)))
59		mux.Handle("GET /profiles/{id}", protect(api.NewGetProfileHandler(deps)))
60	
61		return mux
62	}
63	
64	// stopgapReadOwnAllow builds StopgapAuthorizer's hard-coded (sub ->
65	// allowed record id) map from cfg — empty (denies everything) unless
66	// both AuthzQASub and AuthzQAAllowedProfileID are set, which LT-51 does
67	// once the QA client credential and a matching seeded profile exist.
68	func stopgapReadOwnAllow(cfg config.Config) map[string]map[string]bool {
69		if cfg.AuthzQASub == "" || cfg.AuthzQAAllowedProfileID == "" {
70			return nil
71		}
72		return map[string]map[string]bool{
73			cfg.AuthzQASub: {cfg.AuthzQAAllowedProfileID: true},
74		}
75	}

	}
}

// writeInternalError is writeDAOError's twin for a collaborator that
// isn't the DAO — RateLimiter, TouchCounter, Authorizer — kept as a
// separate, identically-behaving function rather than reusing
// writeDAOError at those call sites (Oren Castellan, PR #26 review):
// writeDAOError is named and documented as the DAO-sentinel translator,
// and a future edit to its switch (adding a case for some DAO-specific
// condition) should not silently also change how an authz/rate-limit
// collaborator's errors are handled. Behavior is identical today — never
// err.Error(), context.DeadlineExceeded still maps to 504 — because none
// of these collaborators have their own sentinel set to translate.
func writeInternalError(w http.ResponseWriter, err error) {
	if errors.Is(err, context.DeadlineExceeded) {
		writeError(w, http.StatusGatewayTimeout, "deadline_exceeded", "request timed out")
		return
	}
	writeError(w, http.StatusInternalServerError, "internal_error", "internal error")
}

Callers\n// that have no working Repository at all route the protected endpoints\n// to this instead of constructing a Deps with a nil Repo (which would\n// panic the first time a handler called d.Repo.Profiles()).\nfunc WriteServiceUnavailable(w http.ResponseWriter) {\n\twriteError(w, http.StatusServiceUnavailable, \"service_unavailable\", \"service temporarily unavailable\")\n}\nEOF\necho done",

2	// bearer-token auth). Per decisions/go-layout-debate.md, this file is a

4	// internal/app and internal/config, not here.

18	
19	func main() {
20		cfg, err := config.Load()
21		if err != nil {
22			log.Fatalf("api-service: loading config: %v", err)
23		}
24	
25		addr := cfg.HTTPAddr
26		if addr == "" {
27			addr = ":8080"
28		}
29	
30		log.Printf("api-service: mode=%s listening on %s", cfg.AppMode, addr)
31	
32		var handler http.Handler
33		switch app.Mode(cfg.AppMode) {
34		case app.ModeVerifier:
35			// The verifying Deployment is the one that talks to the
36			// database (LT-39's search/retrieve handlers) — the issuer
37			// Deployment (below) never needs a Repository at all, since it
38			// only mints tokens.
39			repo, err := dao.New(cfg.DBDriver, cfg.DBDSN)
40			if err != nil {
41				log.Fatalf("api-service: opening DAO repository: %v", err)
42			}
43			handler = app.NewVerifierRouter(cfg, repo)
44		default:
45			// ModeIssuer (and the unrecognized-mode fallback, which
46			// NewRouter itself already logs loudly and treats as verifier —
47			// see app.NewRouter's own doc comment) don't need the full
48			// verifier stack; NewRouter's existing /auth/token placeholder
49			// (LT-32) and /healthz cover both.
50			handler = app.NewRouter("api-service", app.Mode(cfg.AppMode))
51		}
52	
53		if err := http.ListenAndServe(addr, handler); err != nil {
54			log.Fatalf("api-service: %v", err)
55		}
56	}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID-worktrees/LT-40/internal/api/jwks_test.go",
  "content": "package api\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"crypto/rand\"\n\t\"crypto/rsa\"\n\t\"encoding/base64\"\n\t\"encoding/json\"\n\t\"errors\"\n\t\"net/http\"\n\t\"net/http/httptest\"\n\t\"sync/atomic\"\n\t\"testing\"\n\t\"time\"\n)\n\nfunc jwkFromPublicKey(kid string, pub *rsa.PublicKey) jwk {\n\treturn jwk{\n\t\tKty: \"RSA\",\n\t\tKid: kid,\n\t\tAlg: \"RS256\",\n\t\tUse: \"sig\",\n\t\tN:   base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString(pub.N.Bytes()),\n\t\tE:   base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString(bigEndianExponent(pub.E)),\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc bigEndianExponent(e int) []byte {\n\t// Standard RSA public exponent (65537) fits in 3 bytes; encode\n\t// minimally, matching how real JWKS producers encode \"e\".\n\tb := []byte{byte(e >> 16), byte(e >> 8), byte(e)}\n\ti := 0\n\tfor i < len(b)-1 && b[i] == 0 {\n\t\ti++\n\t}\n\treturn b[i:]\n}\n\nfunc jwksBody(t *testing.T, kid string, pub *rsa.PublicKey) []byte {\n\tt.Helper()\n\tdoc := jwksResponse{Keys: []jwk{jwkFromPublicKey(kid, pub)}}\n\tbody, err := json.Marshal(doc)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"marshaling JWKS: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\treturn body\n}\n\nfunc TestParseJWKS_RoundTrip(t *testing.T) {\n\tkey, err := rsa.GenerateKey(rand.Reader, 2048)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"generating key: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tdata := jwksBody(t, \"key-1\", &key.PublicKey)\n\n\tkeys, err := parseJWKS(data)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"parseJWKS: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tgot, ok := keys[\"key-1\"]\n\tif !ok {\n\t\tt.Fatal(\"parsed key set missing key-1\")\n\t}\n\tif got.N.Cmp(key.N) != 0 || got.E != key.E {\n\t\tt.Error(\"parsed public key does not match the original\")\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc TestParseJWKS_SkipsNonRSAKeys(t *testing.T) {\n\tdata := []byte(`{\"keys\":[{\"kty\":\"EC\",\"kid\":\"ec-1\",\"crv\":\"P-256\",\"x\":\"AAAA\",\"y\":\"AAAA\"}]}`)\n\tkeys, err := parseJWKS(data)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"parseJWKS: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tif len(keys) != 0 {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"expected a non-RSA key to be skipped, got %d keys\", len(keys))\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc TestParseJWKS_MissingKidIsError(t *testing.T) {\n\tdata := []byte(`{\"keys\":[{\"kty\":\"RSA\",\"n\":\"AAAA\",\"e\":\"AQAB\"}]}`)\n\tif _, err := parseJWKS(data); err == nil {\n\t\tt.Error(\"expected an error for an RSA key with no kid\")\n\t}\n}\n\n// jwksTestServer serves body while up, or a configurable failure while\n// down — atomic so it's safe to flip from the test goroutine while the\n// cache's own fetches run (any of them) against it.\ntype jwksTestServer struct {\n\tsrv  *httptest.Server\n\tup   atomic.Bool\n\tbody []byte\n}\n\nfunc newJWKSTestServer(body []byte) *jwksTestServer {\n\ts := &jwksTestServer{body: body}\n\ts.up.Store(true)\n\ts.srv = httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {\n\t\tif !s.up.Load() {\n\t\t\tw.WriteHeader(http.StatusInternalServerError)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\tw.Header().Set(\"Content-Type\", \"application/json\")\n\t\t_, _ = w.Write(s.body)\n\t}))\n\treturn s\n}\n\nfunc (s *jwksTestServer) Close() { s.srv.Close() }\n\nfunc TestJWKSCache_FetchesAndCaches(t *testing.T) {\n\tkey, err := rsa.GenerateKey(rand.Reader, 2048)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"generating key: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tvar requests atomic.Int32\n\tbody := jwksBody(t, \"key-1\", &key.PublicKey)\n\tsrv := httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {\n\t\trequests.Add(1)\n\t\tw.Header().Set(\"Content-Type\", \"application/json\")\n\t\t_, _ = w.Write(body)\n\t}))\n\tdefer srv.Close()\n\n\tcache := NewJWKSCache(srv.URL, time.Hour, nil)\n\tctx := context.Background()\n\tif _, err := cache.KeyForKid(ctx, \"key-1\"); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"first KeyForKid: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tif _, err := cache.KeyForKid(ctx, \"key-1\"); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"second KeyForKid: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tif requests.Load() != 1 {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"fetched the JWKS endpoint %d times, want exactly 1 (cached within TTL)\", requests.Load())\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc TestJWKSCache_RefetchesAfterTTLExpires(t *testing.T) {\n\tkey, err := rsa.GenerateKey(rand.Reader, 2048)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"generating key: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tvar requests atomic.Int32\n\tbody := jwksBody(t, \"key-1\", &key.PublicKey)\n\tsrv := httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {\n\t\trequests.Add(1)\n\t\tw.Header().Set(\"Content-Type\", \"application/json\")\n\t\t_, _ = w.Write(body)\n\t}))\n\tdefer srv.Close()\n\n\tcache := NewJWKSCache(srv.URL, time.Minute, nil)\n\tfakeNow := time.Now()\n\tcache.now = func() time.Time { return fakeNow }\n\tctx := context.Background()\n\n\tif _, err := cache.KeyForKid(ctx, \"key-1\"); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"first KeyForKid: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tfakeNow = fakeNow.Add(time.Minute + time.Second)\n\tif _, err := cache.KeyForKid(ctx, \"key-1\"); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"second KeyForKid: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tif requests.Load() < 2 {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"fetched the JWKS endpoint %d times, want at least 2 (TTL should have expired)\", requests.Load())\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc TestJWKSCache_UnknownKidWithNoStaleCacheIsError(t *testing.T) {\n\tkey, err := rsa.GenerateKey(rand.Reader, 2048)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"generating key: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tbody := jwksBody(t, \"key-1\", &key.PublicKey)\n\tsrv := httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {\n\t\tw.Header().Set(\"Content-Type\", \"application/json\")\n\t\t_, _ = w.Write(body)\n\t}))\n\tdefer srv.Close()\n\n\tcache := NewJWKSCache(srv.URL, time.Hour, nil)\n\tif _, err := cache.KeyForKid(context.Background(), \"no-such-kid\"); err == nil {\n\t\tt.Error(\"expected an error for an unrecognized kid\")\n\t}\n}\n\n// TestJWKSCache_StaleCacheSurvivesTransientFetchFailure covers the\n// resilience case within the staleness bound: a fetch failure well\n// inside maxStaleness still serves the last-good key set.\nfunc TestJWKSCache_StaleCacheSurvivesTransientFetchFailure(t *testing.T) {\n\tkey, err := rsa.GenerateKey(rand.Reader, 2048)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"generating key: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tbody := jwksBody(t, \"key-1\", &key.PublicKey)\n\ts := newJWKSTestServer(body)\n\tdefer s.Close()\n\n\tcache := NewJWKSCache(s.srv.URL, time.Minute, nil) // maxStaleness = 3*ttl = 3min\n\tfakeNow := time.Now()\n\tcache.now = func() time.Time { return fakeNow }\n\tctx := context.Background()\n\n\tif _, err := cache.KeyForKid(ctx, \"key-1\"); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"first KeyForKid: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tfakeNow = fakeNow.Add(time.Minute + time.Second) // TTL expired, well within the 3min staleness bound\n\ts.up.Store(false)\n\tif _, err := cache.KeyForKid(ctx, \"key-1\"); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"KeyForKid during a transient fetch failure = %v, want the stale cache to still serve the key\", err)\n\t}\n}\n\n// TestJWKSCache_FailsClosedPastMaxStaleness is the PM's ruling on Tomasz\n// Wrede's HIGH finding: a signing key can be revoked because it's\n// compromised, and if the JWKS endpoint is unreachable during that same\n// incident, an unbounded stale-cache fallback would keep verifying\n// tokens signed by the revoked key for as long as fetches keep failing.\n// Past maxStaleness (3x ttl, per the PM's ruling), the cache must fail\n// closed — ErrJWKSStale — rather than serve the stale set indefinitely.\nfunc TestJWKSCache_FailsClosedPastMaxStaleness(t *testing.T) {\n\tkey, err := rsa.GenerateKey(rand.Reader, 2048)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"generating key: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tbody := jwksBody(t, \"key-1\", &key.PublicKey)\n\ts := newJWKSTestServer(body)\n\tdefer s.Close()\n\n\tcache := NewJWKSCache(s.srv.URL, time.Minute, nil) // maxStaleness = 3min\n\tfakeNow := time.Now()\n\tcache.now = func() time.Time { return fakeNow }\n\tctx := context.Background()\n\n\tif _, err := cache.KeyForKid(ctx, \"key-1\"); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"first KeyForKid: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\ts.up.Store(false)\n\t// Advance past maxStaleness (3min) from the last successful fetch.\n\tfakeNow = fakeNow.Add(4 * time.Minute)\n\t_, err = cache.KeyForKid(ctx, \"key-1\")\n\tif err == nil {\n\t\tt.Fatal(\"expected an error once the cache exceeds max staleness with the issuer still unreachable\")\n\t}\n\tif !errors.Is(err, ErrJWKSStale) {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"error = %v, want it to wrap ErrJWKSStale so the middleware can log a distinct audit event\", err)\n\t}\n}\n\n// TestJWKSCache_UnknownKidTriggersOneRateLimitedRefetch is the PM's\n// ruling on Tomasz Wrede's MEDIUM-HIGH finding: an unknown kid (a key\n// the issuer may have just rotated in) triggers one out-of-band refetch\n// before the cache gives up, rather than waiting up to a full TTL for\n// the next calendar-scheduled refresh.\nfunc TestJWKSCache_UnknownKidTriggersOneRateLimitedRefetch(t *testing.T) {\n\toldKey, err := rsa.GenerateKey(rand.Reader, 2048)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"generating key: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tnewKey, err := rsa.GenerateKey(rand.Reader, 2048)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"generating key: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tvar requests atomic.Int32\n\tvar serveNewKey atomic.Bool\n\tsrv := httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {\n\t\trequests.Add(1)\n\t\tw.Header().Set(\"Content-Type\", \"application/json\")\n\t\tif serveNewKey.Load() {\n\t\t\t_, _ = w.Write(jwksBody(t, \"key-new\", &newKey.PublicKey))\n\t\t} else {\n\t\t\t_, _ = w.Write(jwksBody(t, \"key-old\", &oldKey.PublicKey))\n\t\t}\n\t}))\n\tdefer srv.Close()\n\n\t// A long TTL so the calendar-scheduled refresh would NOT fire on its\n\t// own within this test — proving any successful lookup of \"key-new\"\n\t// came from the unknown-kid-triggered refetch, not an ordinary TTL\n\t// rollover.\n\tcache := NewJWKSCache(srv.URL, time.Hour, nil)\n\tctx := context.Background()\n\n\tif _, err := cache.KeyForKid(ctx, \"key-old\"); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"priming the cache with key-old: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\trequestsAfterPriming := requests.Load()\n\n\t// The issuer rotates: key-new is now published, but the cache's TTL\n\t// hasn't expired.\n\tserveNewKey.Store(true)\n\tif _, err := cache.KeyForKid(ctx, \"key-new\"); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"KeyForKid(key-new) = %v, want the unknown-kid refetch to find it\", err)\n\t}\n\tif requests.Load() <= requestsAfterPriming {\n\t\tt.Error(\"expected an extra fetch triggered by the unknown kid, beyond the priming fetch\")\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc TestJWKSCache_UnknownKidRefetchIsRateLimited(t *testing.T) {\n\tkey, err := rsa.GenerateKey(rand.Reader, 2048)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"generating key: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tvar requests atomic.Int32\n\tbody := jwksBody(t, \"key-1\", &key.PublicKey)\n\tsrv := httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {\n\t\trequests.Add(1)\n\t\tw.Header().Set(\"Content-Type\", \"application/json\")\n\t\t_, _ = w.Write(body)\n\t}))\n\tdefer srv.Close()\n\n\tcache := NewJWKSCache(srv.URL, time.Hour, nil)\n\tfakeNow := time.Now()\n\tcache.now = func() time.Time { return fakeNow }\n\tctx := context.Background()\n\n\tif _, err := cache.KeyForKid(ctx, \"key-1\"); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"priming: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\trequestsAfterPriming := requests.Load()\n\n\t// Two lookups for an unknown kid in quick succession (same fake\n\t// \"now\") must trigger at most one extra refetch between them, not\n\t// one per lookup — that's the rate limit closing the \"unknown-kid\n\t// flood hammers the issuer\" gap.\n\t_, _ = cache.KeyForKid(ctx, \"no-such-kid\")\n\tafterFirstUnknown := requests.Load()\n\t_, _ = cache.KeyForKid(ctx, \"no-such-kid\")\n\tafterSecondUnknown := requests.Load()\n\n\tif afterFirstUnknown != requestsAfterPriming+1 {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"first unknown-kid lookup triggered %d extra fetch(es), want exactly 1\", afterFirstUnknown-requestsAfterPriming)\n\t}\n\tif afterSecondUnknown != afterFirstUnknown {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"second unknown-kid lookup (within the rate-limit window) triggered an extra fetch: %d -> %d, want no change\", afterFirstUnknown, afterSecondUnknown)\n\t}\n}\n"
