1	package api
2	
3	import (
4		"context"
5		"sync"
6		"time"
7	)
8	
9	// GrantLimiter enforces the token-issuing endpoint's own failed-grant
10	// rate limiting and progressive backoff — handoff-03-auth.md v3:
11	// "Token-endpoint brute-force protection, separate from the resource-API
12	// rate limits ... failed-grant rate limiting and progressive backoff per
13	// client_id and per source IP on the token-issuing endpoint itself, plus
14	// alerting on a sustained run of failed grants against one client_id."
15	//
16	// This is genuinely separate from RateLimiter (ratelimit.go): that type
17	// gates the resource API by an already-authenticated sub, at a fixed
18	// per-minute rate; this one gates the endpoint that issues the
19	// credential in the first place, keyed independently by client_id AND
20	// by source IP — a distributed brute force (many IPs, one client_id)
21	// and a single-source credential-stuffing run (one IP, many client_ids)
22	// are both attack shapes this is meant to slow down, and either key
23	// exceeding its own backoff blocks the attempt.
24	//
25	// Not wired to a live endpoint by this story: /auth/token's real grant
26	// logic is LT-51's scope (this story's non-goals). This is the
27	// ready-to-use component LT-51 constructs and calls from the real
28	// handler — building it now, tested in isolation, means LT-51 doesn't
29	// also have to design a backoff/alerting scheme under its own deadline.
30	type GrantLimiter interface {
31		// Allow reports whether a grant attempt for clientID from sourceIP
32		// may proceed right now — false while either key is in backoff.
33		Allow(ctx context.Context, clientID, sourceIP string) (bool, error)
34		// RecordFailure records a failed grant attempt for both clientID and
35		// sourceIP, advancing each key's backoff state independently.
36		// Crossing the alert threshold for a given client_id (not source IP
37		// — the requirement names client_id specifically) fires alert
38		// exactly once per crossing, not once per subsequent failure.
39		RecordFailure(ctx context.Context, clientID, sourceIP string)
40		// RecordSuccess clears backoff state for both clientID and
41		// sourceIP — a successful grant resets the count, standard
42		// exponential-backoff practice: this deters sustained brute force,
43		// it does not permanently degrade a legitimate client that mistyped
44		// a secret once.
45		RecordSuccess(ctx context.Context, clientID, sourceIP string)
46	}
47	
48	// grantBackoffState is one key's (a client_id's, or a source IP's)
49	// consecutive-failure bookkeeping.
50	type grantBackoffState struct {
51		consecutiveFailures int
52		blockedUntil        time.Time
53		alerted             bool
54	}
55	
56	// InProcessGrantLimiter is an in-process implementation. As with
57	// RateLimiter/TouchCounter's in-process implementations, a shared store
58	// is the multi-instance replacement behind the same interface — the
59	// mechanism is this track's call, per handoff-03-auth.md v3.
60	type InProcessGrantLimiter struct {
61		mu             sync.Mutex
62		byClientID     map[string]*grantBackoffState
63		bySourceIP     map[string]*grantBackoffState
64		baseBackoff    time.Duration
65		maxBackoff     time.Duration
66		alertThreshold int
67		alertFn        func(clientID string, consecutiveFailures int)
68		now            func() time.Time
69	}
70	
71	// NewInProcessGrantLimiter constructs a limiter with exponential backoff
72	// starting at baseBackoff (doubling per consecutive failure, capped at
73	// maxBackoff) and alertFn called once per client_id crossing
74	// alertThreshold consecutive failures. alertFn may be nil (no alerting —
75	// tests use this; production wiring passes a real sink).
76	func NewInProcessGrantLimiter(baseBackoff, maxBackoff time.Duration, alertThreshold int, alertFn func(clientID string, consecutiveFailures int)) *InProcessGrantLimiter {
77		return &InProcessGrantLimiter{
78			byClientID:     make(map[string]*grantBackoffState),
79			bySourceIP:     make(map[string]*grantBackoffState),
80			baseBackoff:    baseBackoff,
81			maxBackoff:     maxBackoff,
82			alertThreshold: alertThreshold,
83			alertFn:        alertFn,
84			now:            time.Now,
85		}
86	}
87	
88	func (l *InProcessGrantLimiter) Allow(_ context.Context, clientID, sourceIP string) (bool, error) {
89		l.mu.Lock()
90		defer l.mu.Unlock()
91		now := l.now()
92		return !isBlocked(l.byClientID[clientID], now) && !isBlocked(l.bySourceIP[sourceIP], now), nil
93	}
94	
95	func isBlocked(s *grantBackoffState, now time.Time) bool {
96		return s != nil && now.Before(s.blockedUntil)
97	}
98	
99	func (l *InProcessGrantLimiter) RecordFailure(_ context.Context, clientID, sourceIP string) {
100		l.mu.Lock()
101		defer l.mu.Unlock()
102		now := l.now()
103	
104		cs := l.advance(l.byClientID, clientID, now)
105		l.advance(l.bySourceIP, sourceIP, now)
106	
107		if cs.consecutiveFailures >= l.alertThreshold && !cs.alerted {
108			cs.alerted = true
109			if l.alertFn != nil {
110				l.alertFn(clientID, cs.consecutiveFailures)
111			}
112		}
113	}
114	
115	// advance increments key's consecutive-failure count and sets its
116	// exponential backoff window, creating the state entry if this is its
117	// first failure.
118	func (l *InProcessGrantLimiter) advance(m map[string]*grantBackoffState, key string, now time.Time) *grantBackoffState {
119		s, ok := m[key]
120		if !ok {
121			s = &grantBackoffState{}
122			m[key] = s
123		}
124		s.consecutiveFailures++
125		backoff := l.baseBackoff << uint(s.consecutiveFailures-1) // 2^(n-1) * base
126		if backoff > l.maxBackoff || backoff <= 0 {               // overflow guard: a left-shifted duration can wrap negative
127			backoff = l.maxBackoff
128		}
129		s.blockedUntil = now.Add(backoff)
130		return s
131	}
132	
133	func (l *InProcessGrantLimiter) RecordSuccess(_ context.Context, clientID, sourceIP string) {
134		l.mu.Lock()
135		defer l.mu.Unlock()
136		delete(l.byClientID, clientID)
137		delete(l.bySourceIP, sourceIP)
138	}
139

  "new_string": "// InProcessGrantLimiter is an in-process implementation. As with\n// RateLimiter/TouchCounter's in-process implementations, a shared store\n// is the multi-instance replacement behind the same interface — the\n// mechanism is this track's call, per handoff-03-auth.md v3.\n//\n// Both maps are swept periodically (see sweepLocked) rather than left to\n// grow forever: this component's own doc comment names \"a distributed\n// brute force (many IPs, one client_id)\" as a defended attack shape, and\n// that is exactly the traffic pattern that would otherwise grow\n// bySourceIP without bound — every distinct attacking IP earns a\n// permanent entry that only a matching RecordSuccess would remove, which\n// an attacking IP by definition never triggers (Nolan Reyes, PR #30\n// review — the same unbounded-growth shape an earlier PR's TouchCounter\n// review already found and fixed once).\ntype InProcessGrantLimiter struct {\n\tmu             sync.Mutex\n\tbyClientID     map[string]*grantBackoffState\n\tbySourceIP     map[string]*grantBackoffState\n\tbaseBackoff    time.Duration\n\tmaxBackoff     time.Duration\n\talertThreshold int\n\talertFn        func(clientID string, consecutiveFailures int)\n\tnow            func() time.Time\n\tlastSweep      time.Time\n}\n\n// grantSweepInterval and grantEntryTTL bound how long a key's state\n// survives with no further failures against it. A blocked key is never\n// swept before its own backoff has elapsed (sweeping mid-block would\n// silently un-block it early); grantEntryTTL is generous past any\n// realistic maxBackoff so a legitimately still-escalating attacker isn't\n// reset early either.\nconst (\n\tgrantSweepInterval = time.Minute\n\tgrantEntryTTL      = time.Hour\n)"
}

  "new_string": "func (l *InProcessGrantLimiter) Allow(_ context.Context, clientID, sourceIP string) (bool, error) {\n\tl.mu.Lock()\n\tdefer l.mu.Unlock()\n\tnow := l.now()\n\tl.sweepLocked(now)\n\treturn !isBlocked(l.byClientID[clientID], now) && !isBlocked(l.bySourceIP[sourceIP], now), nil\n}\n\n// sweepLocked drops any entry whose blockedUntil is at least\n// grantEntryTTL in the past — called with l.mu already held, at most\n// once per grantSweepInterval, so it adds no per-call cost beyond a\n// single time comparison on every other call.\nfunc (l *InProcessGrantLimiter) sweepLocked(now time.Time) {\n\tif now.Sub(l.lastSweep) < grantSweepInterval {\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\tl.lastSweep = now\n\tsweepMap(l.byClientID, now)\n\tsweepMap(l.bySourceIP, now)\n}\n\nfunc sweepMap(m map[string]*grantBackoffState, now time.Time) {\n\tfor key, s := range m {\n\t\tif now.Sub(s.blockedUntil) >= grantEntryTTL {\n\t\t\tdelete(m, key)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n}"

	// (each individually under threshold) is a different signal
	// (distributed credential stuffing) this limiter still blocks via
	// the source-IP key, but doesn't claim to alert on by itself.
	var alerted bool
	l := NewInProcessGrantLimiter(time.Nanosecond, time.Nanosecond, 3, func(clientID string, n int) {
		alerted = true
	})
	fakeNow := time.Now()
	l.now = func() time.Time { return fakeNow }
	ctx := context.Background()

	clientIDs := []string{"client-a", "client-b", "client-c"}
	for _, id := range clientIDs {
		l.RecordFailure(ctx, id, "«IP»")
		fakeNow = fakeNow.Add(time.Millisecond)
	}
	if alerted {
		t.Error("alertFn should be keyed on a single client_id's consecutive failures, not fired for varying client_ids sharing a source IP")
	}
}

67	func startFakeJWKS(t *testing.T, kid string, pub *rsa.PublicKey) *httptest.Server {
68		t.Helper()
69		body, err := json.Marshal(map[string]any{
70			"keys": []map[string]string{{
71				"kty": "RSA",
72				"kid": kid,
73				"alg": "RS256",
74				"use": "sig",
75				"n":   base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString(pub.N.Bytes()),
76				"e":   base64.RawURLEncoding.EncodeToString([]byte{1, 0, 1}), // 65537
77			}},
78		})
79		if err != nil {
80			t.Fatalf("marshaling fake JWKS: %v", err)
81		}
82		return httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
83			w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
84			_, _ = w.Write(body)
85		}))
86	}

88	func signTestToken(t *testing.T, key *rsa.PrivateKey, kid, scope string) string {
89		t.Helper()
90		c := jwt.MapClaims{
91			"iss":   testIssuer,
92			"aud":   testAudience,
93			"sub":   "client-under-test",
94			"scope": scope,
95			"exp":   time.Now().Add(10 * time.Minute).Unix(),
96			"iat":   time.Now().Unix(),
97		}
98		tok := jwt.NewWithClaims(jwt.SigningMethodRS256, c)
99		tok.Header["kid"] = kid
100		signed, err := tok.SignedString(key)
101		if err != nil {
102			t.Fatalf("signing test token: %v", err)
103		}
104		return signed
105	}

107	func TestNewVerifierRouter_UnauthenticatedRequest_401(t *testing.T) {
108		key, err := rsa.GenerateKey(rand.Reader, 2048)
109		if err != nil {
110			t.Fatalf("generating key: %v", err)
111		}
112		jwks := startFakeJWKS(t, "key-1", &key.PublicKey)
113		defer jwks.Close()
114

116		router := NewVerifierRouter(cfg, &fakeRepo{})
117	
118		req := httptest.NewRequest(http.MethodPost, "/profiles/search", strings.NewReader(`{"name":"a"}`))
119		w := httptest.NewRecorder()
120		router.ServeHTTP(w, req)
121		if w.Code != http.StatusUnauthorized {
122			t.Errorf("status = %d, want 401 with no bearer token", w.Code)
123		}
124	}

126	// TestNewVerifierRouter_AuthenticatedSearch_ReachesHandler proves the
127	// full stack — deadline middleware, JWT verification against a real
128	// JWKS-over-HTTP fetch, AuthContext population, and LT-39's handler —
129	// is wired together correctly end to end, not just each piece in
130	// isolation.
131	func TestNewVerifierRouter_AuthenticatedSearch_ReachesHandler(t *testing.T) {
132		key, err := rsa.GenerateKey(rand.Reader, 2048)
133		if err != nil {
134			t.Fatalf("generating key: %v", err)
135		}
136		jwks := startFakeJWKS(t, "key-1", &key.PublicKey)
137		defer jwks.Close()
138	
139		cfg := config.Config{AuthJWTIssuer: testIssuer, AuthJWTAudience: testAudience, AuthJWKSURL: jwks.URL}
140		router := NewVerifierRouter(cfg, &fakeRepo{})
141		tok := signTestToken(t, key, "key-1", "profile:search")
142	
143		req := httptest.NewRequest(http.MethodPost, "/profiles/search", strings.NewReader(`{"name":"jane"}`))
144		req.Header.Set("Authorization", "Bearer "+tok)
145		w := httptest.NewRecorder()
146		router.ServeHTTP(w, req)
147		if w.Code != http.StatusOK {

Bash cd /Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID-worktrees/LT-40 && git add -A && git commit -m "$(cat <<'EOF
input
{
  "command": "cd /Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID-worktrees/LT-40 && git add -A && git commit -m \"$(cat <<'EOF'\nfix: JWKS staleness/eviction/context-threading, startup fail-closed, review round 2\n\nFixes both live-issue classes surfaced this round: Oren's/Nolan's PR #30\nreview findings, Tomasz Wrede's (02) cold review findings, and the PM's\ndirect question about deployed startup behavior with no database and no\nreachable JWKS endpoint.\n\nNolan Reyes — production failure modes, both real:\n- JWKSCache.fetch used http.DefaultClient (Timeout: 0, i.e. none) and a\n  bare httpClient.Get with no context — a hung (not refused) connection\n  to the issuer's JWKS endpoint could block a goroutine indefinitely,\n  completely ignoring the 5s deadline middleware's own guarantee. Fixed:\n  fetch now uses http.NewRequestWithContext threading the caller's\n  context through, and NewJWKSCache's default client carries an explicit\n  defaultJWKSClientTimeout (10s) as a backstop independent of context.\n- KeyForKid held c.mu across the entire network fetch, so one hung fetch\n  serialized every other concurrent request through the same cache\n  instance — the whole verifying Deployment's authenticated surface, not\n  just the request that triggered the refresh. Fixed: the lock is never\n  held during I/O; concurrent callers that both decide a refresh is due\n  may both fetch (cheap, occasional duplicate work), which is far\n  preferable to one goroutine blocking every other one.\n- InProcessGrantLimiter's byClientID/bySourceIP maps had no eviction path\n  other than a matching RecordSuccess — the exact traffic pattern this\n  component defends against (a distributed brute force from many source\n  IPs) is precisely what would grow bySourceIP unbounded, since an\n  attacking IP by definition never succeeds. Fixed with the same\n  sweep-on-access pattern TouchCounter already uses (gated so it costs\n  nothing per-call beyond a single time comparison).\n\nTomasz Wrede (02 cold review) / PM ruling — both fixed to the PM's exact\nnumbers:\n- JWKS's stale-cache-on-fetch-failure fallback had no ceiling: if a\n  signing key is revoked because it's compromised, and the JWKS endpoint\n  happens to be unreachable during that same incident, the verifier\n  would keep accepting tokens signed by the revoked key for as long as\n  fetches kept failing. Fixed: JWKSCache now fails closed (ErrJWKSStale)\n  once the cache has gone unrefreshable for longer than\n  jwksMaxStalenessMultiple (3) times ttl, logging once per threshold\n  crossing for 04's alerting. NewJWTMiddleware logs a distinct audit\n  event (AuditJWKSUnavailable, new AuditEventKind) for this specific\n  failure mode rather than folding it into an ordinary auth failure —\n  this is a token-infrastructure degradation, not a bad-credential\n  attempt.\n- An unknown kid previously waited for the next calendar-scheduled TTL\n  refresh, meaning a freshly-rotated key could be rejected for up to a\n  full TTL. Fixed: one rate-limited (jwksUnknownKidRefetchInterval, 30s)\n  out-of-band refetch is attempted before giving up, closing the\n  rotation window from this direction too.\n- StopgapAuthorizer's deny path had zero test coverage, and no router\n  test exercised GET /profiles/{id} under read:own at all. Added\n  stopgap_authorizer_test.go (allow, deny-by-unseeded-id,\n  deny-by-unseeded-sub, empty-map-denies-everything, read:any/search\n  always-allowed) and\n  TestNewVerifierRouter_ReadOwn_AllowedAndDenied (the real router,\n  real StopgapAuthorizer, both outcomes).\n\nOren Castellan — minor findings, both fixed:\n- singleScope counted claim occurrences, not distinct scope values, so\n  a malformed-but-unambiguous \"profile:search profile:search\" claim was\n  rejected as \"more than one,\" contradicting the function's own stated\n  reasoning. Now dedupes by distinct Scope value.\n- bearerToken's \"Bearer \" match was case-sensitive; RFC 7235 §2.1 treats\n  the auth-scheme token as case-insensitive. I have what I need for the review. I re-reviewed the full current state of PR #30 (`gh pr diff 30`, commit `03181f6`). **1. My two original findings — both correctly fixed.** `singleScope()` (jwtmiddleware.go) now dedupes by distinct `Scope` value in a `map[Scope]bool` rather than counting claim occurrences — `"profile:search profile:search"` now falls into the `len(found) == 1` branch and is accepted, only a genuinely distinct second scope trips the "more than one" rejection. Correct. `bearerToken()` now does `strings.EqualFold(h[:prefixLen], "Bearer ")`, with the `len(h) &lt;= prefixLen` bounds check kept ahead of the slice — no panic on a short header, and `bearer`, `BEARER`, etc. now match per RFC 7235 §2.1. Correct. **2. JWKS staleness logic (`refresh()`, `reserveUnknownKidRetry`, the maxStaleness check) — one real issue.** The bounded fail-closed threshold itself is right: `maxStaleness = 3*ttl`, `refresh()` only returns `ErrJWKSStale` once `staleSince &gt; maxStaleness` with the existing cache still in place, and returns nil (serve stale) below that bound — verified against `TestJWKSCache_StaleCacheSurvivesTransientFetchFailure` and `TestJWKSCache_FailsClosedPastMaxStaleness`, both consistent with the code path. The unknown-kid retry is correctly rate-limited to once per `jwksUnknownKidRefetchInterval`, and the lock-not-held-during-fetch fix is real (fetch happens outside `c.mu`, state mutation happens after re-acquiring it). The issue: **there is no backoff on the *normal* refresh path once the cache goes stale.** `KeyForKid`'s `needsRefresh` check is `keys == nil || now.Sub(fetchedAt) &gt;= ttl`. On a failed fetch, `fetchedAt` is never advanced — only a successful fetch updates it. So once `ttl` has elapsed and the JWKS endpoint is down, `needsRefresh` is true on every single subsequent request, and every request calls `refresh()` → a real network fetch, one per request, for as long as the outage lasts (up to `maxStaleness`, then it fails closed — but by then it's already made one blocking HTTP call per authenticated request the whole time). The unknown-kid path got an explicit rate limit for exactly this reason ("Rate-limited so an unknown-kid flood can't be used to hammer the issuer's JWKS endpoint" — the code's own comment), but the ordinary TTL-refresh path has no equivalent throttle. Under load during an outage this is self-inflicted: every request now blocks on a real network round-trip (bounded by `defaultJWKSClientTimeout`/the caller's context deadline, so `deadlineBudget` = 5s per request) instead of failing fast against the cached state, and it keeps hammering the issuer's endpoint at full request volume for the entire outage window rather than backing off. This is worth a follow-up story or at minimum a rate limit on the ordinary refresh attempt itself (e.g., don't retry more than once per some short interval once a failure has been recorded), not a blocker for this PR's stated scope but a real gap in the same "don't hammer a struggling dependency" reasoning the PR already applied elsewhere. **3. New issues from this round's other changes.** Nothing else stood out as incorrect. `GrantLimiter`'s sweep is lock-held and bounded correctly (`sweepLocked`, `grantEntryTTL`), the exponential-backoff overflow guard (`backoff &lt;= 0` catching a left-shift wraparound) is correct, `StopgapAuthorizer`'s default-deny-on-empty-map is verified by its own test, and `main.go`'s `ModeVerifier` nil-repo path plus `NewVerifierRouter`'s 503-not-panic behavior is exercised end-to-end by `TestNewVerifierRouter_NilRepo_ProtectedRoutesFailClosed503`, including confirming auth still runs before the repo is consulted (401 before 503). I did not find a new nullability, off-by-one, or error-swallowing defect in this round's diff. Recommend passing this to Renata with one line: item 2 above (JWKS refresh path has no backoff once stale, unlike the unknown-kid path) is a legitimate gap worth a named follow-up, not a hold on this PR.</result> <usage><subagent_tokens>85956</subagent_tokens><tool_uses>9</tool_uses><duration_ms>59820</duration_ms></usage> </task-notification>

1	// jwks.go implements a minimal RFC 7517 JSON Web Key Set client: fetch,
2	// parse RSA public keys, cache with a short TTL. handoff-03-auth.md v4:
3	// "Public-key distribution for the verifier ... JWKS served by the issuer
4	// Deployment, reachable only in-cluster via its own ClusterIP Service ...
5	// Verifier caches the fetched key set in memory with a short TTL (matching
6	// token TTL is reasonable) to avoid a JWKS fetch on every single
7	// verification."
8	package api
9	
10	import (
11		"context"
12		"crypto/rsa"
13		"encoding/base64"
14		"encoding/json"
15		"errors"
16		"fmt"
17		"io"
18		"log"
19		"math/big"
20		"net/http"
21		"sync"
22		"time"
23	)
24	
25	// jwk is one entry in a JWKS response — only the fields this verifier
26	// needs to reconstruct an RSA public key (RFC 7517 §4, RFC 7518 §6.3.1).
27	// Fields this verifier doesn't use (x5c, x5t, ...) are simply not
28	// unmarshaled; unknown fields in the source JSON are not an error.
29	type jwk struct {
30		Kty string `json:"kty"`
31		Kid string `json:"kid"`
32		Alg string `json:"alg"`
33		Use string `json:"use"`
34		N   string `json:"n"` // base64url, unpadded — the RSA modulus
35		E   string `json:"e"` // base64url, unpadded — the RSA public exponent
36	}
37	
38	type jwksResponse struct {
39		Keys []jwk `json:"keys"`
40	}
41	
42	// parseJWKS decodes a JWKS JSON document into a map of kid -> public key.
43	// Any key with kty != "RSA" is skipped (not an error) — a future non-RSA
44	// key in the set (e.g. during an algorithm migration) shouldn't break
45	// verification of tokens signed with the RSA keys that ARE present.
46	func parseJWKS(data []byte) (map[string]*rsa.PublicKey, error) {
47		var resp jwksResponse
48		if err := json.Unmarshal(data, &resp); err != nil {
49			return nil, fmt.Errorf("api: parsing JWKS: %w", err)
50		}
51		keys := make(map[string]*rsa.PublicKey, len(resp.Keys))
52		for _, k := range resp.Keys {
53			if k.Kty != "RSA" {
54				continue
55			}
56			if k.Kid == "" {
57				return nil, fmt.Errorf("api: JWKS RSA key missing kid")
58			}
59			pub, err := rsaPublicKeyFromJWK(k)
60			if err != nil {
61				return nil, fmt.Errorf("api: JWKS key %q: %w", k.Kid, err)
62			}
63			keys[k.Kid] = pub
64		}
65		return keys, nil
66	}
67	
68	func rsaPublicKeyFromJWK(k jwk) (*rsa.PublicKey, error) {
69		nBytes, err := base64.RawURLEncoding.DecodeString(k.N)
70		if err != nil {
71			return nil, fmt.Errorf("decoding n: %w", err)
72		}
73		eBytes, err := base64.RawURLEncoding.DecodeString(k.E)
74		if err != nil {
75			return nil, fmt.Errorf("decoding e: %w", err)
76		}
77		n := new(big.Int).SetBytes(nBytes)
78		e := new(big.Int).SetBytes(eBytes)
79		if !e.IsInt64() {
80			return nil, fmt.Errorf("exponent out of range")
81		}
82		return &rsa.PublicKey{N: n, E: int(e.Int64())}, nil
83	}
84	
85	// KeySource resolves a kid to the RSA public key that should verify a
86	// token carrying it. JWKSCache is the real implementation; tests use a
87	// map-backed fake. ctx carries the calling request's own deadline —
88	// KeyForKid's real implementation must bound its network I/O by it, not
89	// by some separate, undocumented timeout (Nolan Reyes, PR #30 review: a
90	// hung fetch that ignores the caller's context defeats the deadline
91	// middleware's whole guarantee).
92	type KeySource interface {
93		KeyForKid(ctx context.Context, kid string) (*rsa.PublicKey, error)
94	}
95	
96	// ErrJWKSStale is returned by JWKSCache.KeyForKid when the cached key set
97	// has exceeded its max-staleness bound and could not be refreshed — the
98	// verifier must fail closed rather than trust keys this old (Tomasz
99	// Wrede, 02 cold review; PM ruling). Exported so NewJWTMiddleware can log
100	// a distinct audit event (AuditJWKSUnavailable) for this specific
101	// failure mode, rather than folding it into an ordinary auth failure.
102	var ErrJWKSStale = errors.New("api: JWKS cache exceeded max staleness and could not be refreshed")
103	
104	// jwksMaxStalenessMultiple and jwksUnknownKidRefetchInterval are the
105	// concrete numbers the PM ruled on for Tomasz Wrede's two findings:
106	//   - a signing key can be revoked BECAUSE it's compromised; if the JWKS
107	//     endpoint is also unreachable during that same incident, a
108	//     stale-cache-forever fallback would keep verifying tokens signed by
109	//     the revoked key for as long as fetches keep failing. Bounding
110	//     staleness at a multiple of the normal refresh interval (ttl) turns
111	//     "fail open forever" into "fail open for a bounded window, then
112	//     fail closed" — resilient to a brief blip, not to an extended one.
113	//   - an unknown kid could be a key the issuer rotated in since the last
114	//     TTL-driven refresh; without an out-of-band refetch, a freshly
115	//     rotated key would be rejected for up to a full TTL. Rate-limited
116	//     so an unknown-kid flood can't be used to hammer the issuer's JWKS
117	//     endpoint.
118	const (
119		jwksMaxStalenessMultiple      = 3
120		jwksUnknownKidRefetchInterval = 30 * time.Second
121	)
122	
123	// JWKSCache fetches a JWKS document from url and caches the parsed key
124	// set for ttl, refetching only after it expires — "to avoid a JWKS fetch
125	// on every single verification" (handoff-03-auth.md v4). Supports at
126	// least two concurrently valid kids during a rotation overlap window by
127	// construction: it caches the WHOLE key set the issuer publishes, not
128	// just the most recent key, so an old and a new key both verify
129	// successfully for as long as the issuer's own JWKS response lists both.
130	type JWKSCache struct {
131		url          string
132		ttl          time.Duration
133		maxStaleness time.Duration
134		httpClient   *http.Client
135		now          func() time.Time
136	
137		mu                  sync.Mutex
138		keys                map[string]*rsa.PublicKey
139		fetchedAt           time.Time // last SUCCESSFUL fetch
140		consecutiveFailures int
141		staleAlerted        bool
142		lastUnknownKidRetry time.Time
143	}
144	
145	// defaultJWKSClientTimeout backstops a hung fetch (a connection that
146	// accepts but never responds — not a hard refusal, which fails fast on
147	// its own) when no context deadline is shorter. Distinct from and
148	// independent of a per-request context deadline: this fires even for a
149	// call that somehow reaches fetch() with no deadline on its context at
150	// all, which the http.Client.Timeout field guards against regardless of
151	// context (Nolan Reyes, PR #30 review — the shipped wiring passed nil
152	// for httpClient, resolving to http.DefaultClient, whose zero-value
153	// Timeout means "none").
154	const defaultJWKSClientTimeout = 10 * time.Second
155	
156	// NewJWKSCache constructs a cache fetching from url, refetching at most
157	// once per ttl (and, for an unrecognized kid specifically, at most once
158	// per jwksUnknownKidRefetchInterval outside the normal TTL cycle). A nil
159	// httpClient constructs one with defaultJWKSClientTimeout — never
160	// http.DefaultClient, whose Timeout is unset (no bound at all) by
161	// default. maxStaleness is jwksMaxStalenessMultiple * ttl.
162	func NewJWKSCache(url string, ttl time.Duration, httpClient *http.Client) *JWKSCache {
163		if httpClient == nil {
164			httpClient = &http.Client{Timeout: defaultJWKSClientTimeout}
165		}
166		return &JWKSCache{
167			url:          url,
168			ttl:          ttl,
169			maxStaleness: jwksMaxStalenessMultiple * ttl,
170			httpClient:   httpClient,
171			now:          time.Now,
172		}
173	}
174	
175	// KeyForKid never holds c.mu across a network fetch (Nolan Reyes, PR #30
176	// review, finding #2): the prior shape held the lock for the whole
177	// function body, so one hung fetch stalled every other concurrent
178	// request through this same cache instance — i.e. every authenticated
179	// request the verifying Deployment was serving, not just the one that
180	// triggered the refresh. Concurrent callers that both decide a refresh
181	// is due may both fetch; that's cheap, occasional duplicate work, and far
182	// preferable to serializing the whole authenticated surface behind one
183	// goroutine's network call.
184	func (c *JWKSCache) KeyForKid(ctx context.Context, kid string) (*rsa.PublicKey, error) {
185		c.mu.Lock()
186		needsRefresh := c.keys == nil || c.now().Sub(c.fetchedAt) >= c.ttl
187		c.mu.Unlock()
188	
189		if needsRefresh {
190			if err := c.refresh(ctx); err != nil {
191				return nil, err
192			}
193		}
194	
195		if key, ok := c.lookupKey(kid); ok {
196			return key, nil
197		}
198	
199		// Unknown kid: might be a key the issuer rotated in since the last
200		// TTL-driven refresh. One rate-limited out-of-band refetch before
201		// giving up, so a freshly-rotated key isn't rejected for up to a
202		// full TTL (Tomasz Wrede, 02 cold review). Best-effort: its error is
203		// deliberately ignored here — the outcome that matters is whether
204		// the retry populated the kid we're actually looking for, and a
205		// refresh() failure here has already been handled (logged / staleness
206		// tracked) inside refresh() itself.
207		if c.reserveUnknownKidRetry() {
208			_ = c.refresh(ctx)
209			if key, ok := c.lookupKey(kid); ok {
210				return key, nil
211			}
212		}
213	
214		return nil, fmt.Errorf("api: no key found for kid %q", kid)
215	}
216	
217	func (c *JWKSCache) lookupKey(kid string) (*rsa.PublicKey, bool) {
218		c.mu.Lock()
219		defer c.mu.Unlock()
220		k, ok := c.keys[kid]
221		return k, ok
222	}
223	
224	// reserveUnknownKidRetry reports whether an unknown-kid-triggered refetch
225	// may proceed now, and if so atomically marks one as just having started
226	// — a caller enumerating unknown kids can trigger at most one extra
227	// fetch per jwksUnknownKidRefetchInterval, not one per request.
228	func (c *JWKSCache) reserveUnknownKidRetry() bool {
229		c.mu.Lock()
230		defer c.mu.Unlock()
231		if c.now().Sub(c.lastUnknownKidRetry) < jwksUnknownKidRefetchInterval {
232			return false
233		}
234		c.lastUnknownKidRetry = c.now()
235		return true
236	}
237	
238	// refresh performs the network fetch (no lock held during it) and
239	// updates cache state. Returns an error only when the caller must fail
240	// the whole verification: no cache exists yet (cold start against an
241	// unreachable issuer), or the existing cache has exceeded maxStaleness —
242	// wrapped in ErrJWKSStale in the latter case specifically, so the
243	// middleware can log AuditJWKSUnavailable rather than an ordinary auth
244	// failure. A transient failure within the staleness bound returns nil:
245	// the stale cache is still good enough to use, per the resilience this
246	// mechanism exists for.
247	func (c *JWKSCache) refresh(ctx context.Context) error {
248		fresh, err := c.fetch(ctx)
249	
250		c.mu.Lock()
251		defer c.mu.Unlock()
252	
253		if err == nil {
254			c.keys = fresh
255			c.fetchedAt = c.now()
256			c.consecutiveFailures = 0
257			c.staleAlerted = false
258			return nil
259		}
260	
261		c.consecutiveFailures++
262	
263		if c.keys == nil {
264			return err
265		}
266	
267		staleSince := c.now().Sub(c.fetchedAt)
268		if staleSince > c.maxStaleness {
269			if !c.staleAlerted {
270				c.staleAlerted = true
271				// Log line for 04's alerting pipeline on a prolonged
272				// fetch-failure run — not a policy decision (AuditLogger is
273				// for those), an operational signal that this process's own
274				// key infrastructure is degraded.
275				log.Printf("api: JWKS unrefreshable for %s across %d consecutive failures (exceeds max staleness %s) — failing closed: %v",
276					staleSince, c.consecutiveFailures, c.maxStaleness, err)
277			}
278			return fmt.Errorf("%w: stale for %s across %d consecutive failures: %v", ErrJWKSStale, staleSince, c.consecutiveFailures, err)
279		}
280		return nil
281	}
282	
283	func (c *JWKSCache) fetch(ctx context.Context) (map[string]*rsa.PublicKey, error) {
284		req, err := http.NewRequestWithContext(ctx, http.MethodGet, c.url, nil)
285		if err != nil {
286			return nil, fmt.Errorf("api: building JWKS request for %s: %w", c.url, err)
287		}
288		resp, err := c.httpClient.Do(req)
289		if err != nil {
290			return nil, fmt.Errorf("api: fetching JWKS from %s: %w", c.url, err)
291		}
292		defer func() { _ = resp.Body.Close() }()
293		if resp.StatusCode != http.StatusOK {
294			return nil, fmt.Errorf("api: fetching JWKS from %s: status %d", c.url, resp.StatusCode)
295		}
296		// 1MiB ceiling — a JWKS response is a handful of RSA keys, never
297		// this large; capped so a misbehaving/compromised issuer endpoint
298		// can't force this process to buffer an unbounded response body.
299		body, err := io.ReadAll(io.LimitReader(resp.Body, 1<<20))
300		if err != nil {
301			return nil, fmt.Errorf("api: reading JWKS response: %w", err)
302		}
303		return parseJWKS(body)
304	}
305

  "new_string": "const (\n\tjwksMaxStalenessMultiple      = 3\n\tjwksUnknownKidRefetchInterval = 30 * time.Second\n\n\t// jwksMinRefreshRetryInterval floors how often a TTL-expired cache\n\t// with a just-failed refresh will attempt another real network\n\t// fetch. Short relative to ttl/maxStaleness:\n\t// this only throttles retries during an active failure, it must not\n\t// delay picking up a healthy issuer again once it recovers.\n\tjwksMinRefreshRetryInterval = 5 * time.Second\n)"
